A cold desktop start settles every pending hold as interrupted, since no consult process survives a restart. Do NOT spawn the continuation directly or settle on `onComplete` alone: the queue is what orders it, and a closure is what a reload loses.
- **When a mention runs (parallel / consult first / hand-off):** `inferMentionTiming()` in `src/shared/crossAgentContext.ts`, carried as `CrossAgentMentionPlan.routing`; `armMentionHandoff()`, `releaseMentionHandoff()`, `dropMentionHandoffs()` in `src/renderer/services/crossAgentHandoff.ts`. The order comes from the user's WORDING ("check with @X first", "then send what you find to @X"), so a typed message, a queued edit, and `maestro-cli dispatch` route the same way. `MentionRoutingBar` shows the decision before send, and its mode menu lets the composer user override it for one message: `planCrossAgentMentions(message, id, routingOverride)` applies the pick (`routingSource: 'explicit'`), the override lives in `composerInputStore.mentionRoutingOverride`, and a queued message carries it as `QueuedItem.mentionRouting` via `queuedMentionFields()`, because the drain re-plans from the text. Mode names, summaries and example phrasings live once in `mentionRoutingCopy.ts`. Consult-first is a consult hold with `deferred` set (no local spawn; the release IS the answering turn), and a hand-off is `AITab.pendingMentionHandoff`, released by the exit listener on a clean exit and cleared by Stop. Build the local turn's prompt with `withMentionTurnNotes()`, not `withConsultPendingNote()` alone, or a hand-off turn never learns its answer is forwarded. Write access is per kind: `resolveCrossAgentWritable()` (`src/main/ipc/handlers/cross-agent.ts`) reads `crossAgentHandoffsWritable` (default ON) for a hand-off and `crossAgentMentionsWritable` (default off) for a consult, and a hand-off's prompt uses `handoffGrant()`, never the consult's `cwdGrant()`.
- **An agent asking another agent (`maestro-cli ask`):** `runCrossAgentAsk()` in `src/renderer/services/crossAgentAsk.ts`, over the same `sendCrossAgentRequest()` a typed `@mention` uses. `dispatch` is NOT the verb for a question: it writes into the target's ACTIVE tab, so the question lands mid-conversation in whatever the human has open with that agent, and the answer goes to the screen instead of back to the agent that asked. `--background` does not fix that - it decides where the VIEW lands, not which conversation the prompt joins, so a backgrounded dispatch interrupts QUIETLY, which is worse. The path is `cross_agent_ask` (WS) -> `handleCrossAgentAsk` -> the `consultAgent` callback -> `remote:crossAgentAsk` -> `runCrossAgentAsk`, and the ONLY thing it adds over the mention path is `SendCrossAgentRequestOptions.onComplete`, which hands the finished text to a caller blocked on it. Three rules the resolver encodes: the consult is keyed on the CALLING AGENT plus the constant `CROSS_AGENT_ASK_TAB_ID`, never the caller's active tab, so one consult tab per (caller -> target) pairing survives the agent switching tabs and a follow-up `ask` resumes the earlier thread; **no transcript is forwarded by default** (`ask` sends a self-contained question - `--with-context` opts in, and `buildCrossAgentPrompt` swaps to a header that does not announce a transcript that is not there); and it RESOLVES rather than rejects on every outcome including a missing target, because the caller is a CLI process reporting to an agent and an exception there reads as a broken command instead of "that agent could not answer". `onComplete` is guaranteed to fire exactly once, which is why `completedRequests` remembers the COMPLETION rather than just the id: `crossAgent.send` resolves on a round trip to main and "target agent not found" is emitted before it does, so a bare id set let the `.then()` return having never delivered, and the CLI sat there until its own timeout for a consult that failed instantly. The main-side wait is the caller's timeout, not the dispatch path's 3s delivery receipt - here we wait for an ANSWER, not for the renderer to accept a prompt. See [CLI-UI-PARITY.md](CLI-UI-PARITY.md#ask-is-not-dispatch---background).
- **Marking an agent's own hand-off in its transcript (`dispatch` / `ask` from its shell):** `readCallerIdentity()`, `buildCallerIdentityEnv()`, `callerMessageFields()`, `readCallerMessageFields()`, `isSelfDispatch()` in `src/shared/agentDelegation.ts`; `noteDispatchDelegation()` in `src/main/web-server/handlers/messageHandlers/agentDelegation.ts`; `recordAgentDelegation()` / `settleAgentDelegation()` in `src/renderer/services/agentDelegation.ts`, drawn by `<AgentDelegationCard>` from `LogEntry.delegation`. The failure it replaced: a typed `@mention` shows who was consulted through its reply's attribution header, but an agent that ran `maestro-cli dispatch` or `ask` itself left only a command line inside a tool card. A mention that does not resolve to a known agent is plain text, so the source agent delegates by hand and the same intent was visible one way and buried the other. Invariants: every agent spawn stamps `MAESTRO_CALLER_AGENT_ID` / `MAESTRO_CALLER_TAB_ID` into `effectiveCustomEnvVars` in `handle-spawn.ts` (the session layer, so it survives `STRIPPED_ENV_VARS`, which kills a stale copy inherited from whatever launched Maestro); the CLI forwards them as `fromSessionId` / `fromTabId`, the same wire names `cross_agent_ask` already used; a dispatch pill is recorded only AFTER the renderer accepted the prompt, routed to the CALLER's window; an `ask` records its own pill in `runCrossAgentAsk` as `pending` and settles it when the consult ends, because only the renderer learns the outcome. Traps: do NOT reuse `MAESTRO_AGENT_ID`, which tells `maestro-cli pianola watch` it IS Pianola and switches on its judgment handoff; a named source tab that has closed gets NO pill rather than a guessed tab (the fallback to a lone busy tab, then the active tab, is only for a shell with no tab stamp); and a dispatch into the caller's own tab (or its agent's active tab, with no tab named) is a loop, not a hand-off, so `isSelfDispatch` drops it. The entry is a self-contained card in `isSelfContainedCard`, so streamed output after it starts a new entry instead of being glued onto the pill. CLI tests that assert a message payload must call `isolateAgentEnv([CALLER_AGENT_ID_ENV_VAR, CALLER_TAB_ID_ENV_VAR])`, or they fail when run from inside an agent shell.
- **Running a consult (prompt, budgets, completion, Stop):** `createConsultService()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/agents/consult.ts` is the one place a cross-agent consult is dispatched; `start(request, opts)` builds the prompt (`buildCrossAgentPrompt` in `agents/consult-prompt.ts`), launches the target as an ephemeral `cross-agent-<requestId>` process through a `ConsultRunner`, and settles with exactly ONE terminal chunk. `cancelForSource(sourceSessionId)` is Stop. The host owns only the process: the desktop's runner (`src/main/cross-agent/consult-runner.ts`) is the `ProcessManager` listener code that used to live in the router, and a headless runtime supplies the run layer (`createBackgroundTurns().consultRunner()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/runtime/background-turns.ts`, answered to `maestro-cli ask` by `createRuntimeConsults()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/runtime/consults.ts`). **Rules the service holds so no host re-derives them:** read-only unless `crossAgentMentionsWritable`, and a writable consult asks for FULL access (`permissionMode: 'full'`) rather than merely "not read-only", because `readOnlyMode: false` emits no permission flags and a `--print` run then blocks forever on the first write; the budget is SILENCE (10 minutes, restarted by every `onActivity`) with a separate 30 minute ceiling; a non-zero exit is a failure even when text came back (B17) and a failed or killed run never seeds a resume id (B18); Stop settles `canceled`, not `error`, and lands even before the process exists (the consult registers before its binary is resolved and re-checks around the spawn); an unresolvable SSH remote fails the consult rather than running it locally. A runner attaches its listeners BEFORE it spawns and detaches by itself on a refused or thrown start, so the service never guesses what a failed start left behind, and `stop()` answers the partial output because the buffer is the runner's. The tab a consult writes into is `openConsultTab()` / `recordConsultAnswer()` on the repository: HIDDEN, never active, no tab event, no unread, `saveToHistory: false`, keyed by `consultOrigin`, and the session id is stored only on success. Desktop imports stay at their old paths (`cross-agent-router.ts` re-exports; `useCrossAgentDispatch.ts` re-exports the pure helpers). `toSpawnGroupChatAgentConfig()` in `src/main/group-chat/spawn-config.ts` is the one mapping from a library `GroupChatSpawn` to `spawnGroupChatAgent`, shared by the group chat launcher and the consult runner. The `maestro-cli ask` stand-in ids `CROSS_AGENT_ASK_TAB_ID` / `CROSS_AGENT_ASK_SESSION_ID` are in `src/shared/crossAgentTypes.ts`. Design: `Plans/maestro-tui-group-chat.md` section 7 and 15.
- **Which agents a message mentions, and the `@` picker's rules:** `planMentions()`, `resolveMentionedTargetSessionIds()`, `buildAgentMentionSuggestions()`, `filterAgentMentionSuggestions()`, `buildKnownMentionNameSet()`, `escapeResolvedMentions()`, `consultQuestionOf()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/mentions/roster.ts`, and `getAtMentionTrigger()` / `spliceMentionLiteral()` in `mentions/trigger.ts`. The pure half of cross-agent mentions, over a structural roster (`MentionableAgent`, `MentionableGroup`) so a renderer `Session` and a headless client `AgentRecord` both fit. The renderer's `useAgentMentionCompletion` re-exports from it, and a headless client reads the same rules, so the two cannot disagree about who is mentionable. The desktop consults ANY mention in a message it is handed (`useRemoteHandlers`), so a surface that consults itself must send the source agent `escapeResolvedMentions()` text or the other agent is asked twice. Do NOT hand-roll another `@` scanner or group expansion. The desktop composer plans through `planCrossAgentMentions()` in `src/renderer/services/crossAgentMentions.ts`; `planMentions()` returns the same targets and `routing` (`only`, parallel, consult-first, hand-off), and `mention-plan-parity.test.ts` holds the two to it.

## Auto Run, Cue, Group Chat, Director's Notes

- **Importing a Cue bundle into a data dir:** `planCueBundleImport(options)` (read-only) and `importCueBundle(options)` in `src/main/cue/bundle/cue-bundle-importer.ts`, refusing with `CueBundleImportError` and a stable `code`. Every check runs before the first write: bundle integrity and engine version (`validateCueBundleArchive` over the same bytes `readCueBundleArchive` read), no live Cue engine lock and no live desktop discovery file in the TARGET data dir (`readCueEngineLock(dataDir)`, `isCliServerRunning(dataDir)`), every workspace key mapped to an existing folder, every target contained in its root with no symlink on the way (`assertNoSymlinkOnPath` from `src/main/utils/zip-archive.ts`), and every conflict. Writes go workspace files, Auto Run documents, playbooks, cue.yaml merges, the layout, provider binary paths (`--agent-path`, via `writeAgentConfigsStoreFile`), then `maestro-sessions.json` LAST, and a failed write restores every earlier target from the bytes captured at plan time. Agent records come from `buildNewAgentRecord`; env values go through `sanitizeCustomEnvVars`. Do not write agents or bundle files into a data dir any other way.
- **Merging subscriptions into an existing cue.yaml:** `mergeSubscriptionsIntoCueYaml(raw, subs, { replaceExisting?, settings? })` in `src/main/cue/cue-scheduled-tasks.ts`, written with `writeCueYamlAtomicSync()` from `src/main/cue/cue-yaml-write.ts`. Pure: it returns the new text plus `added` / `replaced` / `conflicts` / `settingsAdded` / `settingsKept`, so a caller can check every workspace before writing any (the bundle importer) or throw on the first shared name (`appendSubscriptionsToYaml`, behind `cue schedule`). Every existing subscription keeps its place and the leading `# Pipeline: ...` comment block survives; comments further down do not, since js-yaml has no comment model, so do not rewrite a file that has nothing to add. Names must stay unique within a file: `removeSubscriptionFromYaml` keys on name alone, so a duplicate would be deleted along with its twin.
- **The GitHub token Cue's own `gh` calls run with:** `buildGhEnv(base, options?)`, `GH_TOKEN_SECRET_NAMES`, `redactGhTokens()` in `src/main/cue/cue-gh-token.ts`. gh never reads a secret file, so a `GH_TOKEN` / `GITHUB_TOKEN` supplied as a systemd credential or Docker secret is looked up with `lookupSecret` and set in the env object of THAT gh call only (never `process.env`: agents inherit it, and in server mode agents must not see Cue's token). Resolved per call so a rotated file is picked up on the next poll; gh's own precedence (`GH_TOKEN`, `GITHUB_TOKEN`, stored login) then applies. A token file that exists but is unusable drops that name from the child env rather than falling back to a stale variable, and is reported by name and path. The poller's `execFileAsync` scrubs every token value from a failure's message, stack, stdout and stderr before anything logs or reports it. Any new place Cue runs gh must go through this, not `getExpandedEnv()` alone.
- **Health of a headless Cue engine (`/healthz`, `/readyz`, `/status`):** `createCueEngineHealth()`, `evaluateLiveness()`, `evaluateReadiness()` in `src/main/cue/cue-engine-health.ts`, served by `startCueStatusServer()` in `src/main/cue/cue-status-server.ts` (`cue engine start --status-port`, 127.0.0.1 only). ONE state object per runner holds the phase (`starting` / `running` / `draining` / `stopped` / `lock-lost`), the last readiness report (re-checked at most once a minute, only when asked) and the event-loop delay window. Everything that changes what a supervisor should be told - the command, the engine's lock heartbeat via `CueEngineDeps.onLockLost`, the SIGTERM drain, the systemd notifier - calls its `mark*()` methods; do NOT add a module global or a parallel flag the server never reads, or `/readyz` reports ready while the engine refuses work. Liveness fails only on a lost lock or a saturated loop (median delay over a 30 s window), never on readiness gaps, so a misconfigured engine is not restart-looped. The server picks every field explicitly: never spread a run, gap or engine object into a body, or a field added later leaks a prompt or payload.
- **Telling someone outside about a headless Cue notification:** `createCueNotifyWebhook()` / `parseNotifyWebhookUrl()` / `buildNotifyWebhookBody()` in `src/main/cue/cue-notify-webhook.ts`, behind `cue engine start --notify-webhook`, fed by `StandaloneCueEngineOptions.onExternalNotification` (notify runs and `auth_expired` classifications). `send` is synchronous and never throws, so a dead endpoint cannot delay a run; POSTs go through `fetchWithTimeout`, at most 4 in flight and 100 queued, no retries, one warning per failure burst. The URL is only ever logged as origin + path, and `user:pass@` becomes a Basic header because `fetch` rejects a URL with credentials. Do NOT add fields to the body by spreading a run or event: pick them, so prompt text, payloads and output stay out.
- **Telling systemd about a headless Cue engine:** `createSystemdNotifier({ env, health, onLog })` / `watchdogIntervalMs()` in `src/main/cue/cue-systemd-notify.ts`. Driven only by the `CueEngineHealth` object (`subscribe`, `statusText`, `noteDrainPhase`), so READY / WATCHDOG / STOPPING cannot disagree with `/readyz` and `/healthz`: the watchdog pings only while `evaluateLiveness` holds. Spawns `systemd-notify` with raw `VAR=VALUE` arguments (no `--stopping`, missing before systemd 253), one child at a time with Node's spawn `timeout`, and strips `NOTIFY_SOCKET` / `WATCHDOG_*` from `process.env` so agents cannot notify on the engine's behalf. Returns null (nothing ever spawned) without `NOTIFY_SOCKET`. Do NOT send a notification from anywhere else; drive it through the health object.
- **Stopping a headless Cue engine without losing work:** `CueEngine.drain({ timeoutMs })` / `forceStop()` in `src/main/cue/cue-engine.ts`, behind `cue engine start`'s SIGTERM/SIGINT and `--drain-timeout`. It is the only stop that keeps acknowledged work: `stop()` deletes the persisted queue, and the desktop keeps using it unchanged. Phases: disarm sources (registry kept), gate the run manager (`setDraining`: queue and persist everything, start nothing), wait for runs and source `settle()`, stop the rest through the stop ladder (recorded `stopped`, never chained), wait for and then kill Cue processes via `deps.countLiveCueProcesses` / `killAllCueProcessesNow` (also waiting for stopped launches that have not spawned yet, `runManager.getUnsettledStoppedRunCount()`; their abort signal keeps them from ever spawning, see `cue-launch-cancel.ts`), re-persist the queue stamped `maestroDrainedAt` (exempt from the restore stale drop), release memory with `discardInMemory()` (not `reset()`) and `fanInTracker.forgetInMemory()` (partial fan-ins are already on disk: the standalone engine writes them through to `cue_fan_in_state` via `cue-fan-in-persistence.ts` and restores them at start). Do NOT add a shutdown path that calls `stop()` or `runManager.reset()` where work should survive, and do NOT hand-roll a kill + `setTimeout`: process stops go through `stopProcess` / `stopAllProcesses` in `cue-process-lifecycle.ts`.
- **Whether a data dir can run Cue unattended:** `checkCueReadiness(inputs)` / `formatCueReadiness()` in `src/main/cue/cue-readiness.ts`, returning `CueReadinessReport` (`ready`, `checkedAt`, counts, `gaps[]` with `kind` + agent/subscription/workspace/secret/tool + an actionable `message`). Behind `cue engine check`, `cue engine start --require-ready` (which refuses BEFORE the engine is built, so nothing is armed), and the status endpoint's `/readyz`. Every gap is collected, never just the first, and each check calls the code the real run calls - `planSessionTurn` (provider, parser, binary at the provider's configured path), `resolveSshLaunchTarget`, `loadCueConfigDetailed`, `resolveSecrets` / `lookupSecret`, `findFanOutTarget` (`src/shared/cue/fan-out-target.ts`, the dispatcher's own fan-out lookup), `isGhInstalled` and `checkBinaryExists` - so readiness cannot pass where a launch would fail for a reason it checks. Tools are probed only when a subscription needs them. Gaps carry names and paths, never a secret value. Do NOT add a readiness rule as a private re-implementation of a launch rule: extend the launch code's exported helper and call it from both.
- **Redacting credentials from text that leaves the machine:** `redactCredentialTokens(text)`, `containsCredentialToken(text)` in `src/shared/agent-run/redact.ts`. The one list of credential SHAPES (OpenAI and Anthropic `sk-`/`rk-` keys including `sk-ant-` and `sk-proj-`, GitHub `ghp_`/`gho_`/`ghs_`/`ghu_`/`ghr_`/`github_pat_`, Slack `xox*-`, AWS `AKIA`), replaced by `[redacted]` with a count. It is precise on purpose, because it runs over prose: a Cue bundle passes Claude memory files, `CLAUDE.md` and skills through it, and those are full of commit SHAs, `token: see GH_TOKEN` lines and words like `sk-learn`, which a looser scanner would mangle. An `sk-` token therefore needs 20+ characters and a digit. `redactPrompt()` in the same file applies this tier FIRST and then its broader prompt-only patterns (any 40-hex blob, `key=value`, `Bearer`), since a ledger prompt can afford to over-redact; before the tier existed its own list missed Anthropic `sk-ant-` keys entirely, because `-` broke its alphanumeric run. For a value you can REFERENCE instead of redact (an env var in `.mcp.json`), replace it with `${NAME}` the way `scrubMcpConfig()` in `src/main/cue/bundle/cue-bundle-claude-assets.ts` does. Do NOT add another prefix list; extend this one.
- **Model tier / effort level (`'low' | 'medium' | 'high'`):** `resolveTierModel()`, `resolveEffortLevel()`, `cheapTurnSettings()`, `cheapResumeTurnSettings()` in `src/shared/modelTiers.ts`; `MAESTRO:MODEL` markers via `findActiveModelHint()` in `src/shared/autorunModelHints.ts`; the join in `resolveTurnSettings()` in `src/shared/autorunTurnSettings.ts`. A marker's PLACEMENT is its scope (own line = from there down, end of a task line = that task only), the two layer per axis, and `'default'` stays a directive rather than collapsing to `undefined` so a task can opt out of a document-wide hint - do NOT normalize it away at parse time. One vocabulary maps to every provider, and it maps to ladder POSITIONS, not same-named strings - Claude's `high` is `max`, Codex's `high` is `xhigh`. Do NOT pass a provider's own effort word through from a document, and do NOT add a tier table for a provider whose model IDs are discovered at runtime (codex, copilot-cli, opencode): a shipped guess rots into naming a model the user cannot run. Unmapped returns `undefined`, meaning inherit-the-agent's-value AND warn - never substitute silently, because a deep-planning task quietly running on the default model is the exact failure this replaced. Shared because the desktop and CLI Auto Run engines must resolve a document identically. Cue subscriptions (`tier` / `model` / `effort` in cue.yaml) ride the same join through `resolveCueTurnSettings()` in `src/shared/cue/turn-settings.ts`; do not resolve a Cue model choice any other way.
- **A provider refusing an effort level:** `parseEffortRejection()`, `noteEffortRejection()`, `getEffortRejection()`, `describeEffortRejection()` in `src/shared/effortRejection.ts`; `rejectedEffortForLevel()`, `isLadderEffortValue()` in `src/shared/modelTiers.ts`; renderer binding `observeEffortRejection()`, `armEffortFallback()`, `isEffortFallbackArmed()`, `settleEffortFallback()` in `src/renderer/services/effortRejection.ts`. The effort ladders in `modelTiers.ts` are a SNAPSHOT of each provider's catalogue, and a provider can retire a rung without notice: Codex dropped `minimal`, and its API answers a retired value with a 400 instead of clamping. Every turn Maestro had picked that value for (tab naming, synopses, context summaries, an Auto Run `effort="low"` hint, a Cue subscription) died before producing a word, and the only trace was a log line. Three pieces keep that from being silent or permanent. **Memory:** the first refusal of a provider and value is noted per PROCESS (main, each renderer, each CLI run has its own copy), and `resolveEffortLevel()` returns `undefined` for a noted value from then on, which every caller already treats as inherit-the-agent's-effort-and-warn. It is keyed by provider and value, NOT by model, because the resolver is asked before a model is known; a value one model refuses is skipped for that provider's other models until restart, which is the safe direction. **Retry:** the turn that DISCOVERS the refusal is run once more without the override, at whichever layer builds the args - `generateTabName` and `groomContext()` in main, `spawnAgentForSession` / `spawnBackgroundSynopsis` in the renderer, `spawnAgent()` in the CLI. Each retries only when it actually SENT an effort, so a refusal of the agent's own configured value cannot loop. **Report:** main's `setupErrorListener` sees every agent error and sends Sentry one message per refused LADDER value (`isLadderEffortValue`), since a stale table is only fixable in a release; the renderer's `useAgentErrorListener` raises one toast. The renderer retry needs a handshake, because the error listener would otherwise park the whole Auto Run behind an error banner for a task that is about to be re-run: the spawn calls `armEffortFallback(processSessionId)` BEFORE spawning, the listener returns early for an armed id, and the spawn's exit handler collects the refusal with `settleEffortFallback()`, which also disarms - so an error that arrives after the exit is never swallowed. Only armed ids are held, so the map cannot grow from spawns with no renderer-side retry. It only ever affects values MAESTRO chose: an effort the user picked on a tab or an agent is never rewritten, and that turn keeps failing with the provider's own message. `parseEffortRejection()` knows the one wording observed in the field (OpenAI's `Unsupported value: 'x' is not supported with the 'y' model` beside `"param":"reasoning.effort"`) and REQUIRES the parameter name, because the API uses the same sentence for other parameters. A provider that phrases its refusal differently is not recognized and fails as before: add its pattern there, never at a call site.
- **A synopsis turn (the cheap summary resumed on a finished session):** the model rule is `cheapResumeTurnSettings(toolType, current)` in `src/shared/modelTiers.ts`; the CLI turn is `requestCliSynopsis(session, agentSessionId, request)` in `src/cli/services/synopsis.ts`. A synopsis RESUMES the session it summarizes, so the provider replays the whole transcript into whatever model the synopsis runs on. `cheapTurnSettings()` alone is therefore not enough to spawn one: swapping an agent on Anthropic's 1M beta (`opus[1m]`) down to the cheap 200k model makes the synopsis of a long conversation fail with "Prompt is too long". `cheapResumeTurnSettings` keeps the session's model whenever the downgrade would shrink the context window, still drops effort to the bottom rung, and applies the inherit-the-agent's-value fallback for providers with no tier mapping, so a caller passes its result straight to the spawn. Pass it what the summarized turn RAN at (a run override or document hint, not just the agent default), because that is the model whose transcript is resumed. The desktop's `spawnBackgroundSynopsis` (`src/renderer/hooks/agent/useAgentExecution.ts`) and the CLI helper both call it; the guard was written inline in the desktop first and then copied into `send`, while CLI Auto Run had no guard at all. `requestCliSynopsis` is the one CLI synopsis spawn, shared by `send` and Auto Run (`playbook`, `run-doc`). It returns `null` for every unusable result - a failed turn, a bare provider error (`isFailedSynopsisResponse`; `claude -p` prints the API error and exits 0, so it arrives as a successful response), or NOTHING_TO_REPORT - and the caller keeps its own summary, so an error line is never written to History as the description of the work. It forwards `readOnlyMode`, because the synopsis runs on the same session as the turn it summarizes and a read-only turn must not be followed by one holding full permissions. Do NOT hand-roll another `spawnAgent(..., PROMPT_IDS.AUTORUN_SYNOPSIS, agentSessionId, ...)` block. `goal-runner.ts`'s hand-off blurb is a different prompt whose output feeds the next iteration, and is deliberately not a synopsis.
- **Un-parking an Auto Run stopped on an error:** `registerBatchResumer()` / `getBatchResumer()` in `src/renderer/services/batchResumer.ts`, the policy in `src/shared/autorunAutoResume.ts` (`resolveAutoResumePolicy`, `autoResumeEnabled`), and the timers in `src/renderer/stores/autoRunResumeStore.ts` (`scheduleAutoResume`, `cancelPendingAutoResume`, `clearAutoResume`). Resolving the run's in-memory error-resolution promise with `'resume'` is the ONLY thing that continues a paused run, and the function that does it lives inside a React hook tree, so every automatic resumer has to be handed the callback - hence one registry, registered once by App. THREE mechanisms can now resume a run and they are ordered, not parallel: Agent Resilience (`retryStore`) claims classified upstream failures on its own backoff; Auto-Resume on Limit (`useAutoResumeCoordinator`) claims quota pauses on an hour-scale probe; this store is the last resort for everything else, on a fixed per-run interval with an attempt ceiling. The error listener only reaches it when `willAutoRetryBatch` was false, and `scheduleAutoResume` refuses limit errors outright - two timers resolving the same promise resume once and then resume an already-running loop. Two traps: attempts are counted PER RUN (a run failing a different way each time must still exhaust its ceiling), and a resume must NOT clear the count - use `cancelPendingAutoResume` from the manual resolve/skip paths and reserve `clearAutoResume` for a run starting or ending, or the ceiling resets every attempt and a broken run auto-resumes forever. Do NOT add this as a third `RetryStrategy`: those carry provider-outage meaning into the transcript card copy, the Resilience dashboard, and the `resilience_events` table.
- **Auto Run markers (HITL / halt / model hint):** `scanMaestroMarkers()`, `findPendingHitlGate()`, `acknowledgeHitlGate()`, `detectHaltMarker()`, `hasMaestroMarker()` in `src/shared/autorunMarkers.ts`, rendered by `remarkMaestroMarkers` (`src/renderer/components/Markdown/remarkMaestroMarkers.ts`) + `<MarkerPill>`. All three markers are HTML comments, so they render as NOTHING - and two of them silently block the next run (a live HITL gate pauses it, a halt marker makes Auto Run refuse to start), which presents to the user as "I pressed Run and nothing happened". `scanMaestroMarkers` is the rendering side and reports each marker's STATUS (`live` / `spent` / `invalid`), because presence is not the useful question: a gate above an unchecked task and one above a checked task are a character apart in the source and only the first stops the run. Do NOT hand-roll another marker regex - the engines and the pill must agree, or the pill sends the user to remove the wrong marker. Pills are wired ONLY into `createMarkdownComponents` (the document map); chat builds its own map, so an agent explaining the syntax in a message keeps rendering as prose rather than claiming a setting exists. One deliberate asymmetry: `detectHaltMarker` is NOT fence-aware (the engine must never miss a halt an agent mis-indented) while `scanMaestroMarkers` is (a pill on a documentation example would be a lie).
- **Auto Run steering notes (mid-run course correction):** `formatSteeringNotesBlock()`, `normalizeSteeringNoteText()`, `MAX_PENDING_STEERING_NOTES` in `src/shared/autorunSteering.ts`; both note lists in `src/renderer/stores/autoRunSteeringStore.ts`; the operator-facing half (`submitSteeringNote`, `cancelSteeringNote`, `takeSteeringNotesForDispatch`, `recordSteeringResponse`) in `src/renderer/services/autoRunSteering.ts`; the ONE surface that sends a note, `ThoughtStreamPanel`. A run spawns a FRESH agent per task, so there is no conversation to interrupt: a message typed during a run would queue a whole separate turn in a context the next task never saw. A steering note instead rides in front of the next task's prompt inside the `MAESTRO:CONDUCTOR-NOTES` block, which `src/prompts/autorun-default.md` (step 8) briefs the agent about so it knows the channel exists before it ever sees one. **A note is a property of the RUN, so it never touches an AI tab.** The first cut routed it through the chat composer - a plain write-mode message during a run was silently re-read as a steering note, and it wrote a transcript entry with a compass badge - which made a message addressed to the agent mean something else, in the one place a user is entitled to assume it does not. Do NOT reintroduce a composer path, a `LogEntry` field, or a transcript badge for this; the panel's **Steer Auto Run** button is the whole gesture, and the pending/delivered strip under its bar is the record. That bar rests as two labelled buttons (Search, Steer) and swaps to whichever input was clicked; steering was a fourth dim icon in the header before, and was not being found. The button is gated on `steeringBlocker(batchRunStates[sessionId])` (running and not mirrored): notes are renderer state, so a run owned by another Maestro client would never read them, and a Steer button that quietly did nothing is worse than no button. **The CLI half is `maestro-cli steer-auto-run send | list | cancel`**, one `auto_run_steering_command` message with an `action` (`src/shared/autoRunSteeringCommands.ts`), routed to the window that OWNS the agent and answered by `runRemoteSteeringCommand()` - which calls the same `submitSteeringNote` / `cancelSteeringNote` the panel does and asks the same `steeringBlocker`, so a click and a CLI call cannot disagree. Three things the wire adds: an over-long note is REFUSED rather than trimmed (the panel caps as the operator types and they see it; a caller across a socket would have a note cut mid-sentence), `list` answers after the run has ended and reports `steerable` instead of failing, and replies travel grouped by delivery so a response shared by several notes is not repeated per note. Four more invariants: the block is prepended AFTER template substitution (a `{{VAR}}` the operator typed is their literal text, not a variable); `takeNotes` reads-and-clears in one store write so a note is delivered exactly once - a note sent between the take and the spawn belongs to the NEXT task; pending and delivered are TWO records rather than one list with a status field, so no read site can forget the filter and offer Cancel on a note a task already read; and a refusal (the pending cap) is announced with a center flash, since the pending strip is the only other evidence a note landed and a silent `false` reads as a dead Send button. Notes live in their own store on purpose: `batchRunStates` is rewritten wholesale by the runner's debounced flush, which would silently drop a note added mid-flush. **A delivered note carries the reply of the task that took it** (`recordSteeringResponse` -> `recordResponse`, called for every settled task including one that threw), because a check mark only says the note reached a prompt. Every note one task took shares a `deliveryId`, and a view renders from `groupSteeringDeliveries()` so that one reply is drawn once under its notes. Lifetimes differ on purpose: PENDING notes die with the run (`clearPendingSteeringNotes` at `COMPLETE_BATCH`), DELIVERED notes survive until the next run starts (`clearSteeringNotes`), since the reply to a run's last note lands as the run ends and a note asking the run to stop ends it. Nothing is persisted. Distinct from **chat steering**, which types into a live PTY mid-turn - same word, no shared mechanism.
- **Steering a turn that is ALREADY RUNNING:** the wire protocol in `src/shared/chatSteering.ts` (`SteeringVerdict`, `parseSteeringRequest` / `parseSteeringResult`, `classifyQueueOperation`, `STEERING_SOCKET_ENV_VAR`), the screen gate in `src/maestro-p/steering-screen.ts` (`editorIsAcceptingInput`, `describeBlockingDialog`, `screenShowsTypedText`), the channel in `src/maestro-p/steering-server.ts` + `TuiDriver.inject()`, the client in `src/main/process-manager/steering-client.ts`, and the UI rule in `getSteerEligibility()` / `shouldOfferSteer()` (`src/renderer/utils/executionQueue.ts`). **Distinct from Auto Run steering** (a separate feature of the same name, landing on rc via back-merge, whose module sits beside this one in `src/shared/`): that one rides a note in front of the NEXT task's prompt because an Auto Run spawns a fresh agent per task, while this types into a PTY that is mid-turn. They share the word and no mechanism, so they stay separate modules. Only a LOCAL maestro-p turn can be steered - `claude --print` closes stdin and holds no writable PTY, and an SSH turn's TUI is on another host - so `apply-local-interactive-spawn.ts` names the socket and that IS the decision; do not re-derive "is this steerable" anywhere else. For `dynamic` mode read `session.claudeInteractive.mode` (the RESOLVED value the spawner writes), never the configured token mode, which is identical in both states. **Absorption is a property of the TURN SHAPE, not of the channel**: claude folds mid-turn input into a loop that still has iterations left and records `reason: "absorbed_mid_turn"`, but a text-only turn parks it and runs it as a follow-up, so every verdict is OBSERVED from the transcript rather than assumed. The screen gate is an ALLOWLIST (the editor box must be drawn) because every blocking dialog REPLACES it, and a blocklist of dialog wording would need to know every dialog claude ever ships - one miss puts an Enter on "1. Yes" and grants the action while destroying the user's message. `inject()` is deliberately not `send()`: no quiet-screen settle (a streaming screen is never quiet), exactly ONE Enter (never the retry burst), and the screen checked before typing AND before submitting. An item with images is NOT steerable - images arrive as `@/path` mentions translated from stdin, which is closed by then, so typing the text alone would silently discard the attachment. The same rule refuses any item whose dispatch does more than send its text: a consult hold (`awaitingConsult`, its text is a placeholder), an unfired `@mention` (`crossAgentMention`, the consult fires only in `processQueuedItem`), and an item carrying `agentContext` (consult replies prepended only at spawn). `inject()` types `normalizeInjectedText()` (CR/CRLF to LF, tabs to spaces) and is SERIALIZED, and verdicts match claude's rows via `steeringTextMatches()` (whitespace-insensitive), because claude records a tab as four spaces and two simultaneous steers otherwise merge into one message. A steer landing after `end_turn` becomes a fresh prompt with no `queue-operation` rows, so it is settled from the `user` entry as `queued`, and the end-turn grace is held open (`STEER_VERDICT_HOLD_MS`) while a delivered steer is unanswered. A steer is answered TWICE: the socket says `delivered`, and the transcript verdict arrives later on stdout, which `StdoutHandler` forwards as `process:steering-verdict`. `steerQueuedItem()` / `applySteeringVerdict()` / `settlePendingSteersOnExit()` in `src/renderer/services/chatSteering.ts` take the item out of the queue BEFORE the socket call (so a turn that ends mid-wait cannot also dispatch it) and hold it out until then, parked on `Session.pendingSteers` (PERSISTED, so a reload between the two answers cannot lose the only copy; `reconcilePendingSteers()` settles any record whose process is gone once the store loads) with a user bubble appended to the tab so the steered message is in the transcript (taken back out if the item returns): `dropped` puts it back at the head live, and an exit with no verdict puts it back PAUSED (never sent twice without asking). `maestro-cli queue steer <item-id> --agent <id>` rides the SAME `steerQueuedItem()` over `steer_queue_item` -> `remote:steerQueueItem`, returning its `QueueSteerResult`; never route a CLI steer straight to `process:steer`, which would type the text and leave the card queued to send twice. Codex steers only on the OPT-IN app-server transport (`codexTransport: 'app-server'`, resolved by `resolveCodexTransport()`); `codex exec` stays the default and is NOT steerable (`codex queue` is accepted and ignored by a running exec turn), so `getSteerEligibility()` blocks it with `codex-exec`. On app-server, `ProcessManager.steer()` routes to `steerCodexAppServerTurn()` (`src/main/codex-app-server/steering.ts`), which sends `turn/steer` against the active turn id and answers with the SAME frames: `delivered` on RPC success, `refused`/`not-running` for no active turn or a turn-id mismatch, `unknown` otherwise; the later verdict is `absorbed` when the translator reports a matching `userMessage` in that turn and `dropped` when the turn ends or the child exits without one. An app-server agent runs app-server on its SSH remote too (the launch rides the ssh client's stdio, see CODEX-APP-SERVER.md "SSH remotes"), still runs image turns on exec, and main answers a steer there `refused`/`not-running` rather than the renderer re-deriving routing. See [CODEX-APP-SERVER.md](CODEX-APP-SERVER.md). Steer is refused for a paused item (an unconfirmed steer pauses it, and steering it again would type it twice), an item waiting for a reconnect, an SSH Claude agent (`ssh-remote`; an SSH Codex agent on app-server steers), while a steer of the same item is still unanswered (`steer-in-flight`), for a read-only message unless the running turn is known to be read-only too (`read-only`: steering cannot narrow the permissions a turn was spawned with), and for a Claude message starting with `/` or `!`, which the TUI would read as a slash command or bash mode (`tui-command-prefix`). The toasts name the provider via `getAgentDisplayName()`, never "Claude". In maestro-p the socket env var is stripped from the TUI's children, a server never unlinks a socket something still answers on, Windows uses a `\\.\pipe\` name, a steer that was refused after typing is cleared from the editor (Ctrl+U, verified on screen) before the next one types, the pre-Enter check re-requires the editor and looks for the typed text INSIDE the editor box, `send()`'s retry Enter taps are cancelled once the turn starts, typed text is stripped of ANSI and C0 controls, and finalize waits for in-flight injects (then reports `dropped` for every unsettled steer) before `/quit`. The Codex translator ignores notifications for any thread but its own, so a sub-agent thread cannot end or misroute the parent turn. The **Steer Running Turn** chord (`steerSend`, default Opt+Cmd+Enter) and its palette entry both call `requestSteerSend()`, which fires `STEER_SEND_EVENT` at the composer (`runSteerSend` in `useInputKeyDown.ts`): with a draft it calls `processInput(undefined, { steer: true })`, which queues the message as usual and then steers THAT item, so a message that cannot be steered is queued rather than lost; with an empty draft it calls `steerNewestQueuedMessage(sessionId, tabId)`. Both end in `steerQueuedItemOrExplain()`, which is `steerQueuedItem()` plus a toast worded by `describeSteerBlocked(reason)` - the Steer button is hidden for every blocked reason and so never says why, but a chord has no control to hide. Do NOT add a second path that types a draft straight into `process:steer`: only a queued item has the park/return bookkeeping that keeps a message from being lost or sent twice. The steered bubble is stamped `LogEntry.steered` by `steeredLogEntry()`, which also starts it at `steerState: 'pending'` (`SteerBubbleState` in `src/shared/chatSteering.ts`); `settlePendingSteers()` is the one place that closes a parked steer the agent TOOK, and it stamps `absorbed` or `queued` on the bubble as it drops the record, so a new settle path must go through it rather than filtering `pendingSteers` by hand (that leaves the bubble spinning forever). `LogItem` draws `<SteeredMessageHeader>` on the bubble and, when `steerJoinsRunningTurn(entry)` in `src/renderer/utils/logEntries.ts` holds, indents it behind a `<SteerTurnConnector>` (both in `src/renderer/components/SteeredMessageHeader.tsx`); a `queued` steer ran as its own turn and is deliberately NOT stitched. The connector lives inside its own row because rows are `content-visibility: auto`. Both flags leave with the bubble when the item returns to the queue, so do not set them anywhere else. **Group chat** steering rides the same `ProcessManager.steer()` but owns its own bookkeeping, because a group chat's queue lives in MAIN: `prepareGroupChatSpawn()` names the socket through its `steeringSocketPath` dep (local maestro-p turns only, never SSH), `pickGroupChatSteerTarget()` in `src/shared/groupChatSteering.ts` is the one target rule (exactly ONE turn: the moderator's if running, else the one working participant the message @mentions, or the only one working; never a broadcast, so there is no partial delivery to reconcile), the engine's `steerRunningRound()` / `steeringVerdict()` / `unsettledSteersOnExit()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/groupchat/router.ts` type it and record it as a user line with a `|steered:` log field (a verdict or exit that beats the socket reply is buffered in `steersAwaitingSocket`, not dropped), and `steerQueuedItem()` / `steerNewMessage()` / `returnSteeredMessage()` in `src/main/group-chat/group-chat-queue.ts` hold the SAME send latch as the drainer, so a round that ends mid-steer cannot also deliver the item as a new message. The Steer button, `groupChat:queueSteer`, and `maestro-cli group-chat steer` (`steer_group_chat`, answered by main directly) all land there; do not steer a group chat from the renderer.
- **Fence-aware markdown scanning:** `forEachMarkdownLine()`, `UNCHECKED_TASK_REGEX`, `CHECKED_TASK_COUNT_REGEX`, `CHECKED_TASK_REGEX` in `src/shared/markdownTaskScan.ts`. Every Auto Run document scanner (task counting, HITL gates, model hints, human-step detection) rides this one walk, in `shared/` because the CLI engine cannot import from `src/renderer`. Do NOT hand-roll another line loop: any scanner that forgets the fence bookkeeping fires on a playbook that merely DOCUMENTS the marker syntax, and the copies drift on closing-fence length, tilde fences, and CRLF.
- **Auto Run document names (absolute path to folder-relative):** `resolveAutoRunDocumentName()`, `resolveAutoRunDocumentNames()` in `src/shared/autoRunDocumentPaths.ts`. Every runner (the CLI's `readDocAndCountTasks()`, the desktop's `useDocumentProcessor`) reads `${folderPath}/${filename}.md`, so a stored playbook or a launched run must name each document relative to the Auto Run folder and without `.md`. The CLI's `auto-run` and Cue `autorun` subscriptions send absolute `.md` paths; the `maestro:configureAutoRun` listener converts them for BOTH `--save-as` and `--launch`. The save branch once skipped the conversion and stored absolute paths, so every saved playbook reported "No unchecked tasks". A document outside the folder is refused by name; do NOT bring back the basename fallback, which silently stored or ran a different (or missing) file.
- **Ordering the Auto Run run list from the picker:** `applySelectionOrder(documents, selectedFilenames, createEntry)`, `selectFolderFiles(prev, files)`, `deselectFolderFiles()`, `selectAllDocuments()` in `src/renderer/utils/documentSelectionOrder.ts`. The run list is an ordered program - document 1 runs, then document 2 - and the "Select Documents" picker is where that order is authored, so selection order IS run order. The picker used to emit `[...documentsAlreadyInTheList, ...newlySelected]`, which pinned anything already added to the front: a user who reopened the picker and clicked folders in the order they wanted them to run got the old order back with the new folders bolted on the end. `applySelectionOrder` rebuilds the list from the selection alone, reusing existing entries so ids, reset flags, and duplicate rows survive and every duplicate stays grouped with its original. `selectFolderFiles` deletes before re-adding so a folder's documents land as one contiguous block at the end even when one of them was already picked individually - without that, clicking a folder leaves a stray document behind at an earlier position. Do NOT rebuild the list from `allDocuments` or the document tree: both are alphabetical, and the user's click order is the only place the intended run order exists.
  <!-- doc-refs-ignore -->
- **Whether an Auto Run is parked waiting on the user:** `useAutoRunErrorPaused(sessionId)` in `src/renderer/hooks/batch/useAutoRunPause.ts`. `batchRunState.isRunning` stays true through a pause, so a surface that asks "is a run active?" to decide whether to lock the document locks it during the exact pause that exists to let the user edit: an agent error the user has to fix, or a `MAESTRO:HITL` gate answered by ticking the box the marker sits above (or by Done, Resume, which writes that box via `acknowledgeHitlGate()`). Split the two questions - `isRunning` means the run is alive (so Stop stays offered), `errorPaused` means it is idle and asking (so the editor and the preview checkboxes open back up). Read the flag through this hook, NEVER off the `batchRunState` prop: the chain store -> `useBatchProcessor` -> `useBatchHandlers` -> `App` -> `RightPanel` -> `AutoRun` drops `errorPaused` updates through `updateBatchStateAndBroadcast`/`UPDATE_PROGRESS`, so a prop read reports `false` for a run that is plainly paused on screen. (The legacy `src/web/mobile` surface this rule was also written for read `autoRunState.errorPaused` straight off the wire; that tree is retired, and the browser now runs the same renderer through the web-desktop bundle, so it goes through the hook like every other surface.)
- **Folding a disk change into an Auto Run draft:** `reconcileDiskContent({ draft, saved, incoming, diskWins })`, `isAutoRunDocumentLocked()`, `isAutoRunRunDocument()` in `src/renderer/utils/autoRunDraft.ts`. The folder watcher re-reads the selected document on every `.md` change, including the echo of the user's own Cmd+S, and both draft layers (`RightPanel`'s shared draft and `useAutoRunContentSync`) used to reset to disk on every tick: text typed after a save, or while an agent touched the file, vanished. The rule: disk equal to the saved baseline is a no-op; a clean draft adopts the disk; a dirty draft is kept and only the baseline moves (Revert then loads the new version); `diskWins` (a run is driving the document) restores the old overwrite so a read-only editor tracks the agent. Both layers MUST call this one function with the same lock answer, or the child and parent disagree and the draft flips on the next render. Only `RightPanel` raises the conflict toast, since the panel and the expanded modal can both be mounted.
- **Auto Run percent complete and ETA:** `autoRunProgressPercent()`, `estimateAutoRunEta()`, `AUTO_RUN_ETA_MIN_TASKS` in `src/shared/autorunProgress.ts`, drawn by `<AutoRunProgressStats>` (`src/renderer/components/AutoRun/AutoRunProgressStats.tsx`). The bar width used to be an inline ternary in `RightPanel`, so a percent label beside it would have been a second copy free to disagree. Pace is active elapsed time (`autoRunActiveElapsedMs`, sleep and pauses excluded) over tasks completed IN THIS RUN: `completedTasksAcrossAllDocs` starts at the documents' pre-checked count, so `BatchRunState.initialCompletedTasks` (set by `START_BATCH`, carried on the broadcast and the web-desktop mirror) is subtracted first, or a run that resumed a half-checked playbook reports a pace several times too fast. A state with no baseline (an older build) gives no ETA rather than a wrong one. No ETA for goal runs or before `AUTO_RUN_ETA_MIN_TASKS` tasks; a looping run's ETA covers the current pass only, and the UI says so.
- **Which agent an Auto Run executes in (worktree targets):** `resolveAutoRunDispatchTarget(launchingSession, config)` in `src/renderer/services/autoRunDispatchTarget.ts`, over `spawnWorktreeAgentAndDispatch()` in `src/renderer/utils/worktreeSpawn.ts`. A run launched "in a worktree" executes in a worktree agent that is already open (`existing-open`), on disk but closed (`existing-closed`), or not created yet (`create-new`), while its documents stay in the LAUNCHING agent's Auto Run folder. Three launch paths need that answer: the Auto Run window's Go button (`useAutoRunHandlers`), the remote `maestro:configureAutoRun` listener behind `maestro-cli auto-run --launch`, and a scheduled run fired by Cue. Before the resolver the Go button handled all three modes while the remote path handled `create-new` only, which is why a scheduled run could not target a worktree at all. It returns a result rather than toasting for an `existing-open` failure because the callers disagree on purpose: the Go button falls back to the active agent (the user is right there) or asks for a retry, while a remote or scheduled launch FAILS - nobody is at the keyboard, and quietly running in the launching agent's own checkout is the one outcome a worktree target exists to prevent. It mutates `config.worktree` when a pull request was asked for, since the path and branch are only known once the worktree is resolved. The cue.yaml spelling (`auto_run.worktree`, snake_case) lives in `src/shared/cue/autorun-worktree.ts`: `parseCueAutoRunWorktree()` is the one validator (the Cue config validator, the normalizer, the YAML writer and `maestro-cli cue schedule` all call it, and an unusable block is an ERROR, never "no worktree"), and `runTargetFromCueWorktree()` / `cueWorktreeFromRunTarget()` are the only conversions to and from `WorktreeRunTarget`. A to-be-created worktree gets `CUE_AUTORUN_WORKTREE_LAUNCH_TIMEOUT_MS` in `src/main/cue-autorun-launcher.ts`, because the renderer accepts only after the setup script finishes and a premature "failed" keeps the schedule on disk to be fired a second time. Do NOT re-inline the three-mode branch at a new launch site.
- **Scheduled Tasks (clock-driven Cue subs):** types and pure helpers in `src/shared/cue/scheduled-tasks.ts`, filesystem work in `src/main/cue/cue-scheduled-tasks.ts`. `maestro-cli cue schedule` and the Cue modal's Scheduled Tasks tab (via `cue:*ScheduledTask*` IPC) both go through that one module, so they cannot write different YAML for the same task. Do NOT hand-roll another cue.yaml writer.
- **Editing one Cue subscription in place:** `patchSubscriptionInYaml(projectRoot, name, mutate)`, `setSubscriptionTurnSettings()`, `findSubscriptionsByName()` in `src/main/cue/cue-subscription-edit.ts`; `applyCueTurnSettingsPatch()` in `src/shared/cue/turn-settings.ts`. One read-mutate-write path for a subscription that already exists: it keeps the file's leading `# Pipeline:` comment header, writes atomically, and never forces a reload (the engine's YAML watcher picks the change up; forcing one races it). `mutate` edits the raw record and returns a refusal reason or `null`, and a refusal writes NOTHING, so a half-applied patch cannot reach disk. `updateScheduledTask()` rides it, and so does `setSubscriptionTurnSettings()`, the single function behind `maestro-cli cue model` and the Scheduled Tasks form's Model / Effort selectors. Tier and exact model are one choice with two spellings: `applyCueTurnSettingsPatch()` removes one when the other is set, and uses `null` (not `undefined`) for "clear" because the patch crosses IPC as JSON. `findSubscriptionsByName()` reads a cue.yaml shared by several agents ONCE; counting it per agent reports a single subscription as ambiguous. Do NOT hand-roll another `yaml.load` / mutate / `yaml.dump` sequence for a cue.yaml edit.
- **Whether two subscriptions are the same visual trigger:** `triggerGroupKey(sub)` in `src/shared/cue/trigger-group-key.ts`. The pipeline serializer writes fan-out to mixed or command targets as SEVERAL parallel subscriptions that each re-carry the whole trigger config, so "one trigger" is a derived identity, not a stored id. Three surfaces need that identity and must agree: `yamlToPipeline` collapses matching subs onto one trigger node, `cue-engine` fires every sibling when the user clicks Play (so a manual run matches a scheduled tick), and `SessionsTable` picks one representative per group for Run Now. They previously held three hand-mirrored copies, and the two runtime copies had already drifted from the editor's - they omitted `retrigger_on_comments` / `max_notifications`, so Play fired subs a real tick would have treated as separate triggers. Adding a new event-config field means adding it HERE, once: leave it out and two subs that differ only in that field silently collapse into one. Do NOT re-inline a JSON.stringify of the trigger fields at a new call site.
- **How big a pipeline is on the canvas:** `pipelineCardBounds(nodes, opts?)`, plus `nodeFootprintWidth()`, `estimateNodeWidth()` and `NODE_CHROME_OVERHANG` in `src/renderer/components/CuePipelineEditor/utils/nodeFootprint.ts`. Three places need a pipeline's on-screen rect and they must agree: the renderer that sizes the translucent group card (`convertToReactFlowNodes`), the drag handler that stops two dropped pipelines landing on each other (`resolveNonOverlappingPipelineOffset`), and the masonry packer that deals cards into columns (`arrangePipelineGroups`). They used to disagree - only the packer sized a node from its text, while the other two assumed a flat `NODE_BG_WIDTH` - so a node with a long name (a shell step named after the script it runs) rendered outside the card that was supposed to contain it, gear, handle, and "in: <agent>" pill included. A node is bigger than the box ReactFlow positions in two ways: its width is text-driven (`width: max-content`), which `estimateNodeWidth` derives from the same text the node components render and errs WIDE on purpose; and its chrome hangs OUTSIDE that box, since handles are centred on the left and right borders and the badges are pinned past a corner, which `NODE_CHROME_OVERHANG` allows for. Do NOT size a card from `NODE_BG_WIDTH` (it is a FLOOR, not a maximum) and do NOT fold the overhang into `PIPELINE_GROUP_PADDING` - the padding is a visual choice someone may want to tune, and tuning it must not be able to clip a handle.
- **Which pipelines belong to an agent:** `pipelinesForSession(sessionId, pipelines, graphSessions)`, plus `pipelineInvolvesSession()` and `pipelineNamesDeclaredBySession()` in `src/renderer/components/CuePipelineEditor/utils/pipelineMembership.ts`. "Belongs" has two independent senses and the Cue dashboard needs both: PARTICIPATION (an agent node bound to the session, or a command node whose `owningSessionId` is the session) and DECLARATION (the pipeline is defined in that agent's own `cue.yaml`, even when no node points back at it - a fan-out that only dispatches to other agents, or an `action: command` pipeline that is just a trigger plus a shell step). The predecessor, `getPipelineColorForAgent()`, answered a COLOR question and checked agent nodes only, which broke twice over: a command-only pipeline belonged to nobody, so the Sessions table printed a dash and "View in Graph" dropped the user into every pipeline on the machine; and because two pipelines can legitimately share a color, pairing a color list back to names dropped one and mislabeled the other. Membership is resolved by pipeline identity, never by color - `PipelineDot` is keyed on `p.id` for the same reason. Declaration matches on `getPipelineKey(sub)` because names are the grouping key `subscriptionsToPipelines` builds with, so they equal `CuePipeline.name` exactly. Do NOT reintroduce a color-based lookup, and do NOT filter agent nodes inline at a new call site.
- **What a group chat does with a message it cannot send yet:** the pure rules in `src/shared/groupChatQueueModel.ts` (`mustQueue`, `canDrain`, `enqueueMessage`, `completeItem`, `failItem`, `failHead`, `requeueFailedAtFront`, `resumeQueue`, `removeItem`, `reorderItem`, `parseQueueState`), driven by `src/main/group-chat/group-chat-queue.ts`. **The queue is owned by MAIN, not by a client.** It used to be a zustand array in the renderer, so every client had its own: a message queued on a phone was invisible to the desktop, died with the browser tab holding it, and was delivered only if that one client happened to observe the moderator going idle. Main is also the only party that can decide whether a message is sent or queued - a client decides from a copy that is already stale, and a stale copy sends directly while items are waiting, putting the NEWEST message in front of older ones, which is the one thing a queue exists to prevent. The invariant the whole module holds: a message the user typed is delivered, or it is still queued with the chat paused and the user told why; there is no third outcome. So a failed send KEEPS the item, marks it, pauses, and posts a system message, and never retries on its own - a cause that does not clear (the Encore Feature off, a missing binary) would otherwise re-fire on every idle forever. Three traps the model encodes so no caller re-derives them: completion and failure are recorded BY ID rather than by position (the send is awaited and the queue is editable while it is in flight, so "drop whatever is first now" discards a message the moderator never received); `enqueueMessage` RESUMES rather than merely un-pausing, because `canDrain` refuses a failed head and a queue that reports itself running while sending nothing is a worse stall than a visible pause; and `parseQueueState` THROWS on a malformed file rather than degrading to empty, so the caller can quarantine the bytes instead of letting the next save erase them. The renderer holds only a MIRROR - `groupChatQueues` in `groupChatStore`, keyed by chat id and written from nothing but the `groupChat:queueState` broadcast and the `getQueue` read a chat performs when it opens, since a client can have been asleep, reloaded, or never seen the chat before. It has NO drain: a renderer-side drain only runs while that client is awake and watching, so a phone that slept left its messages queued forever and two clients that both saw idle each sent the same item. `GroupChatInput` adapts the mirrored items to the `QueuedItem` shape `QueuedItemsList` already speaks, so main never learns a renderer type, and it renders the pause: a paused queue sends nothing, so it has to say so and offer Resume rather than leaving messages sitting there with no explanation and no control. Do NOT add a second queue in a renderer store, and do NOT decide send-vs-queue on the client.
- **How much work happened in a group chat:** `computeGroupChatActivity(entries)` in `src/shared/groupChatActivity.ts`, fed by the per-turn measurements `src/main/group-chat/group-chat-turn-metrics.ts` stamps onto each history entry. **Never present the span between a chat's first and last message as effort** - a group chat is a ROOM, not a task, so a chat used twice a week reports hundreds of hours without anyone doing anything (a real one read `481h 34m` for about five hours of work). Working time is the UNION of the busy intervals, because participants run in PARALLEL: three agents working the same ten minutes is ten minutes of chat time, not thirty, and summing per-agent durations produces a number larger than the chat's own age. Entries written before per-turn timing existed carry no `elapsedTimeMs` and degenerate to points, so `GROUP_CHAT_ACTIVITY_STITCH_MS` treats turns closer than five minutes as one block - without it an older chat reports `0m`, which is a worse lie than the span it replaced. Turn duration is measured with `beginSleepAwareSpan()`, never a bare `Date.now()` delta. `turnsWithTokens` / `turnsWithCost` are coverage counts: render a dash rather than a zero when they are 0, since a chat whose turns reported no usage is UNKNOWN, not free. Both the Group Chat Info overlay and the HTML export call the one function, so they cannot disagree about how much work a chat did.
- **Bucketing Director's Notes bullets:** `bucketNarrativeItems()` / `shouldRenderBuckets()` / `buildNarrativeGroupLookup()` in `src/shared/directorNotesGrouping.ts`, bound to live store state by `useNarrativeGroupLookup()` in `src/renderer/components/DirectorNotes/`. The synopsis agent emits a flat list of bullets per section, each tagged with the session it came from; the GROUP that session belongs to is Maestro's answer, never the model's, which is why it is derived from `sessionStore` instead of being one more field the model can invent. Two surfaces render the same narrative - Rich Mode (`NarrativeSections`) and the markdown path behind Plain Mode / Copy / Save (`narrativeToMarkdown`) - so the bucketing rule lives in one pure module they share; a second copy drifts on the parts that are easy to get subtly wrong: an agent name is matched through `normalizeAgentKey` (the manifest hands the model a sanitized display name, so it rarely matches a stored name byte for byte), a blank `agent` string means the same thing as no agent, the unattributed catch-all always sinks last, the agent pill survives only inside a GROUP bucket (under an agent header it just repeats the header), and a section with ONE bucket stays a flat list because a lone subheading only restates the section title. Do NOT add a `group` field to the prompt contract in `src/prompts/director-notes.md` - the model's job ends at naming the session.
- **Which agent a persisted group chat participant came from:** `findSessionForParticipantName(participantName, sessions)` in `src/shared/group-chat-types.ts`, over `findUniqueMentionMatch()`'s `minPriority` floor. A participant record keeps a NAME and a PROVIDER TYPE (`agentId` is `claude-code`, not a Maestro agent id), so the name is the only link back to the agent the participant runs as. The lookup is deliberately stricter than a typed @mention: it accepts only exact, legacy, and safe-normalized matches (priority 2 and up), because the weakest tier collapses bracket styles (`Review Bot [Linux]` and `Review Bot (Linux)`) and would borrow the wrong agent's cwd, custom args, or SSH config. A tie returns `undefined` rather than picking one. The group chat router calls it to pick the agent a participant runs as, and anything else that must attribute a participant's process to an agent calls the same function, so the two cannot disagree. Do NOT hand-roll another name loop, and do NOT read `participant.agentId` as an agent id.
- **Running an Auto Run with no desktop (launch, progress, pause, stop):** `createRuntimeAutoRun()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/runtime/autorun.ts`, which the in-process `MaestroClient` answers `autoRun.*` through, over the ONE engine the CLI also runs (`runPlaybook()` / `runGoal()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/autorun/`). Surface differences are data, not forks: `CLI_AUTORUN_POLICY` and `DESKTOP_AUTORUN_POLICY` (`autorun/policy.ts`) decide gates (skip or pause), agent errors (record or pause), the synopsis source, whether the document is inlined into the prompt, and goal checkpoint commits; the runtime uses the desktop's. Four rules to keep: a refusal is a `rejected` answer BEFORE anything starts; a run holds its agent's working tree, so a chat message that would write queues until it ends (`autoRunHolds` into `createRuntimeTurns`, `holdsTree` on the queue); a run's turns start through `createAutoRunTurnRunner()` (`assembleAutoRunTurn()` over `assembleTurn()`, `runAgentTurn`, the idle watchdog, one `query_events` row with `source: 'auto'`), never a second spawn path; and its busy marker is `cli-activity.json` UNDER THE RUNTIME'S DATA DIRECTORY (the activity functions take one), never the user's default file. The task and goal synopsis rules are `extractTaskSynopsis()` / `extractGoalSynopsis()` in `autorun/synopsis.ts`, imported by the desktop's two hooks. Do NOT add another Auto Run loop or a second `auto_run_sessions` insert: the SQL is `stats/auto-run-insert.ts`, shared with `src/main/stats/auto-run.ts`. See `Plans/maestro-tui-autorun-engine.md` section 4.8.
- **Running a group chat or a consult with no desktop (rounds, `@participant`, `ask`):** `createRuntimeGroupChats()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/runtime/group-chats.ts` and `createRuntimeConsults()` in `src/shared/maestro-lib/runtime/consults.ts`, which the in-process `MaestroClient` answers `groupChats.*` and `consults.*` through, over `createBackgroundTurns()` (`runtime/background-turns.ts`) and the group chat engine (`createGroupChatEngine()`, `groupchat/router.ts`). **One launch, two hosts:** `prepareGroupChatSpawn()` (`groupchat/spawn.ts`: SSH wrap, Claude token source, Windows shell, turn clock) decides how a turn starts and `planPipeSpawn()` (`run/pipe-spawn.ts`) decides the process, and the desktop's `spawnGroupChatAgent` and `ChildProcessSpawner` call the SAME two, so a turn starts alike on either host; do NOT hand-build another arg chain or stdin rule for one. A group chat or consult process registers under an owner that is NOT an agent id (`group-chat:<chatId>`, `consult:<requestId>`), so shutdown and the fence stop it and no agent reads busy because of it. A finished turn is reported to the engine as `sessionAnnounced`, `usageReported`, then `turnEnded` (`turnEndOf()` in `groupchat/turn-end.ts` builds the report: the answer is the reply, session-not-found is read from the stream tails, a participant that crashed with stderr noise and no answer is closed out silently); the order is load-bearing because the next spawn reads the stored session id. The desktop's session-id, usage and raw-stdout listeners call the same engine functions. **Rules the runtime holds:** a chat takes one round at a time (`send` is refused, never queued: no library client queues), Stop ends the round and any Auto Run the chat started, a participant's `!autorun` runs through the Auto Run service and closes the participant out with `groupChatAutoRunSummary()`, a consult is read-only unless `crossAgentMentionsWritable`, consults that share a consult tab run one at a time while different agents run at once, and the caller's `timeoutMs` and Stop on the asking agent both end a consult. The wire is the desktop bridge's own (`groupChatToWire()` and `framesForEvent()` in `server-frames.ts`, the cases in `server-requests.ts`), and `planGroupChatStart()` in `src/shared/groupChatRemote.ts` is the one set of start checks the renderer and the runtime share. Tests replace `RuntimeDeps.background.runTurn` with `createFakeGroupChatProvider()` (`src/__tests__/shared/maestro-lib/run/fakeGroupChatProvider.ts`). Do NOT add a second spawn path, queue, or consult dispatch for a library client. See `Plans/maestro-tui-group-chat.md` sections 6, 7 and 16.

## Notifications, modals, and surfaces

- **Toast notifications:** `notifyToast({ color, title, message, dismissible? })` in `src/renderer/stores/notificationStore.ts`. Use for async results, errors, and persistent/dismissable messages. Same five-color design language as Center Flash: `green | yellow | orange | red | theme` (default `theme`). Set `dismissible: true` (or pass `--dismissible` from `maestro-cli notify toast`) when the user MUST acknowledge - disables auto-dismiss, requires click to close, and emphasizes the X button. Cannot combine `dismissible` with `duration`/`--timeout`. External CLI cap: 60 seconds (use `--dismissible` for sticky). **Click actions** (data-driven, survive the IPC bridge): pass `clickAction` for what should happen when the toast body is clicked, or use the legacy `sessionId`/`tabId` fields for plain agent jump. The shape and its validator (`parseToastClickAction`) are canonical in `src/shared/toastClickAction.ts` and aliased by every producer (web-server types, preload, `global.d.ts`, `cue-notify-bridge`); the renderer dispatches every kind through `dispatchToastClickAction()` in `src/renderer/services/toastClickActions.ts`. Kinds: `jump-session` (AI tab), `open-file` (File Preview tab), `open-terminal` (focus an existing terminal tab by id or name; omit `tabRef` for the agent's active one), `open-browser` (focus an in-app browser tab by `tabId`, or open `url` in a new one), `open-url` (system browser). Adding a kind is a change in exactly those two files. From the CLI: `--agent` (+ optional `--tab`), `--open-file <path>`, `--open-terminal [tab]`, `--open-browser <url>`, `--open-browser-tab <id>` (all requiring `--agent`), `--open-url <url>` - mutually exclusive with each other. `--action-url` / `--action-label` render an inline link button beneath the message and are independent of the body click. **Source-agent label:** pass `sourceAgent` (CLI `--source-agent <label>`) to stamp which agent/pipeline fired the toast in the header strip. It's store-independent, so it shows even when the agent isn't loaded in the Left Bar (the name resolved from `--agent`/`sessionId` only renders when that agent is in the desktop store) - use it for cron/watchdog toasts. The explicit label wins over the resolved name for display; pair it with `--agent` to also get click-to-jump. Do NOT pass renderer-only callbacks across the bridge - use `clickAction` instead. Native click actions use the same contract: `registerNotificationsHandlers()` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/notifications.ts` buffers clicks until a renderer registers `onClickAction` and signals `notification:ready`; window recreation uses its `ensureMainWindow` dependency.
- **Whether in-app toasts are on:** `areToastsEnabled(duration)`, `TOAST_DURATION_OFF`, `DEFAULT_TOAST_DURATION`, `resolveEnabledToastDuration(previous)` in `src/shared/toastDuration.ts`. One number decides both whether floating toasts render and how long they stay: `-1` is off (notifications still land in the notification center when that feature is on), `0` is until dismissed, a positive value is seconds. The notification store, the inbox, Settings, the bell popover and the command palette all read the sentinel from here instead of hard-coding `-1`. Flip toasts with `setToastsEnabled(enabled)` in the settings store, or `toggleInAppToasts()` in `src/renderer/services/inAppToasts.ts` from a command (it acknowledges with a center flash, because a toast confirming toasts are off would be suppressed by the setting it reports). `setToastDuration` remembers the duration it switches off FROM in `toastDurationBeforeOff`, whichever surface turned them off, so switching back on restores a hand-picked `5s` or `Never` rather than the factory default. Do NOT compare `toastDuration` / `defaultDuration` against a literal `-1`.
- **What a toast click does:** `ToastClickAction` + `parseToastClickAction()` in `src/shared/toastClickAction.ts`, dispatched by `dispatchToastClickAction()` in `src/renderer/services/toastClickActions.ts`. The shape is data-only so it survives the IPC bridge, and every producer (web-server types, preload, `global.d.ts`, `cue-notify-bridge`) aliases the canonical type rather than restating it. Adding a click kind is a change in exactly those two files - a producer that declares its own union compiles fine and then hands the renderer a kind nothing dispatches, so the toast silently does nothing when clicked.
- **Toast history / unread state:** `history`, `markNotificationRead()`, `selectUnreadNotificationCount()` in `src/renderer/stores/notificationStore.ts`; the Left Bar bell and app-level popover in `src/renderer/components/NotificationCenter.tsx` preserve history after floating toasts expire, gated on the `notificationCenterEnabled` setting (mirrored into `config.historyEnabled`; off records nothing). Manual dismissal marks entries read; expiry does not. The shared wire data and mutation reducer are in `src/shared/notificationHistory.ts`; `src/main/ipc/handlers/notification-history.ts` commits deltas and broadcasts snapshots across windows. CLI operations use the live store through `notify inbox` and `open notification-center`, awaiting durable acknowledgement for mutations.
- **Center flash (rapid acks):** `notifyCenterFlash({ message, color, detail?, duration? })` in `src/renderer/stores/centerFlashStore.ts`; clipboard helper `flashCopiedToClipboard()` in `src/renderer/utils/flashCopiedToClipboard.ts`. Use for momentary "I did the thing" confirmations of user-initiated actions. Five-color design language: `green | yellow | orange | red | theme` - default `theme` matches the active Maestro theme. External integrations can fire flashes via `maestro-cli notify flash <message> --color <color>`. Do NOT roll your own center-screen overlay, useState+setTimeout flash, add a sixth color, or use a Toast for clipboard acks. Single visible flash at a time, themed frosted-glass card mounted once in `App.tsx`. Full decision rules, color palette, and design language: [UI-PATTERNS.md → Center Flash System](UI-PATTERNS.md#center-flash-system-rapid-temporary-notifications).
- **Keeping toasts off a surface the user works in:** `useToastAvoidZone()` (callback ref), `toastBottomInset(zones, stack, viewportHeight)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useToastAvoidZone.ts`. In a bottom corner (the `toastPosition` setting, `src/shared/toastPosition.ts`), with the side bar on that side closed or narrower than the toast, the corner is the composer, so a toast covered the text being typed. A surface attaches the ref, its rect lands in `useToastAvoidZoneStore`, and `ToastContainer` lifts the whole stack above any zone it overlaps HORIZONTALLY. The overlap test is the point: a zone the stack does not cross moves nothing, so an open side bar keeps toasts at the window bottom. Top corners skip the check. It is a callback ref (not a `RefObject`) because the composer mounts conditionally, and it re-measures on window resize as well as `ResizeObserver`, since a taller window moves the composer without resizing it. Do NOT add a per-surface bottom offset to `ToastContainer`; register the surface instead.
- **Opening a modal / dashboard by name:** `UI_SURFACES` + `resolveUiSurface()` / `resolveUiSurfaceTab()` / `describeSurfaceAccess()` in `src/shared/uiSurfaces.ts`, dispatched by `openUiSurface()` in `src/renderer/utils/openUiSurface.ts`. One registry serves `maestro-cli open <surface> [--tab]`, the main-process `open_modal` validator, and the renderer dispatcher, and it carries the discovery hints (hotkey id, command-palette text, click target) so an agent can teach the manual path while opening the pane. Adding a modal means adding one entry, not touching four files - and if it has tabs, list them there so `--tab` can deep-link.
- **Whether a modal takes the window over:** `DESTINATION_MODALS`, `closeOtherDestinations()`, and `registerExternalDestination()` in `src/renderer/stores/modalStore.ts`. A DESTINATION is a full-window view you GO to (Settings, Usage Dashboard, Director's Notes, Symphony, Cue, Marketplace, Process Monitor, System Logs, Agent Sessions, Memory, plus the Document Graph); only one is ever open, and `openModal`/`toggleModal` evict whichever other one was up. Before this, ordering was decided by each surface's fixed rank in `MODAL_PRIORITIES` instead of by what the user just asked for: the Usage Dashboard (540) opened BEHIND Director's Notes (848), and a main-panel destination opened under any overlay changed nothing on screen - both read as a dead keystroke. A dialog that answers a question ABOUT the surface beneath it is NOT a destination and is meant to layer (confirmations, rename prompts, `cueYamlEditor`, the dashboard's per-agent detail, the Symphony agent picker). Do NOT try to fix a "wrong surface won" bug by re-ranking `MODAL_PRIORITIES` - decide membership here instead. A destination owned by another store (currently only the Document Graph in `fileExplorerStore`) joins via `registerExternalDestination()` and calls `closeOtherDestinations()` on its own way in; `modalStore` must never import that store back. `DESTINATION_SHORTCUT_IDS` in the same file is the second half of that membership question and is DERIVED from it: the window keyboard handler blocks most shortcuts while a modal is up, and consults this set to decide which hotkeys still get through. It used to be a hardcoded chord test (Alt+Cmd plus `l`/`p`/`u`/`s`), which let three destinations through and killed the rest - Director's Notes to Usage Dashboard worked because `Opt+Cmd+U` matched the chord, and the way back did nothing because `Cmd+Shift+O` did not, so switching between two full-window surfaces worked in one direction only and read as a dead key. Deriving the set from `UI_SURFACES` means adding a destination cannot silently drop it back out of the guard, and a user who REBINDS a surface keeps a working hotkey, which a chord test can never promise. Do NOT add a chord back to that guard to fix a "hotkey does nothing over an open surface" bug - give the surface a `shortcutId` in `UI_SURFACES` instead.
- **Opening a modal from inside Settings:** `launchFromSettings(open)` in `src/renderer/utils/launchFromSettings.ts`. Settings renders at `z-[9999]`, so a launcher inside it (Extensions -> "Open Pianola", a plugin's `modal` panel) opens the new modal BEHIND Settings and the click looks like a no-op. This closes Settings first, then runs the launcher. Not for confirmations or pickers that are meant to stack on top of Settings.
