See [UI-PATTERNS.md](UI-PATTERNS.md#modals-launched-from-inside-settings-launchfromsettings).
- **Modal layer:** `useModalLayer()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useModalLayer.ts` (do NOT use manual `registerLayer()` boilerplate)
- **Whether a surface is the topmost layer:** `useIsTopLayer(priority)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useIsTopLayer.ts`. Matching is by `MODAL_PRIORITIES` value rather than layer id, because `useModalLayer` owns the registration and never hands the id back. Reach for it whenever a surface binds an unmodified key or wants to reclaim focus (`AutoRunExpandedModal`) - do NOT re-derive `getLayers()[length - 1]` inline.
- **Modal / find-bar ESC pill:** `<EscCloseButton>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/EscCloseButton.tsx`. Every modal, palette, and find bar needs a graphical exit - Escape alone strands users on remote desktop and tablets. Pass an `onClose` that does exactly what the Escape handler does (extract it into a shared `useCallback` rather than duplicating the body). Do NOT hand-roll the `px-2 py-0.5 rounded text-xs font-bold` pill; the inert `<div>` version used to be copy-pasted in nine places. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Every Modal Needs a Graphical Exit](UI-PATTERNS.md#every-modal-needs-a-graphical-exit-escclosebutton).
- **A modal default sized to the screen:** `viewportModalSize({ width, height })` in `src/renderer/utils/modalSizing.ts`, passed as `defaultSize` to `useResizableModal`. Only for surfaces the user pans around INSIDE (a graph, a dashboard) - a form or dialog has a natural width and more room buys it nothing. A fixed default that reads as generous on a laptop is a postage stamp on a 5K display, and the user re-drags it on every machine. Memoize it once per mount: the hook re-clamps the live size on `resize`, and a default that moves under it fights that listener.
- **Audio/video playback:** media NEVER becomes a file preview tab. `handleOpenFileTab()` (`src/renderer/hooks/tabs/internal/useFilePreviewTabHandlers.ts`) is the single choke point every open path funnels through, and it diverts playable media to `useMediaPlaybackStore.openMedia()` (or `enqueueMedia()` when the caller passes `mediaMode: 'queue'`) before a tab can be created. Any path that opens SEVERAL files must pass `mediaMode: 'queue'` for every media file after the first - see `openFilesInOrder()` in `useFileContextMenu.ts` - or each open steals the player from the one before and only the last survives. The only surface media appears on is `FloatingMediaPlayer` - there is no docked/in-panel placement, so do NOT add one. Exactly ONE player exists app-wide, owned by `MediaPlaybackHost` (`src/renderer/components/MediaPlayback/`), mounted once in `App.tsx` and never unmounted: anything rendered per-tab or per-agent is torn down on switch, and removing a media element from the document pauses it. Do NOT mount a second player to play two files at once - the single-player invariant is what makes overlapping audio impossible; `stepMediaItem()` walks the queue in open order, a finished file hands off via `advanceAfterEnded()`, and the history menu jumps by recency. **Queue and history are two lists with opposite lifetimes:** the queue persists (settings key `mediaPlayerQueue`, debounced writes, hydrated in `settingsStore`) while history is per-boot, so history holds whole `MediaItem`s rather than IDs into the queue - never "simplify" it back into pointers, or dropping a queue entry would rewrite what the user already heard. **"Open the player" is one store action, `openPlayer()`**, not a sequence a caller reassembles: the command palette entry and its shortcut both call it, and it resolves `selectMediaPlayerTargetId()` at INVOKE time so a queue that advanced while the palette sat open cannot strand it on a finished file. There is exactly ONE palette entry for it - a second, "Show Floating Media Player", used to appear beside it whenever the widget happened to be minimized, and two commands a word apart that both mean "put the player on screen" is a choice the user has to stop and read for a distinction that is internal bookkeeping. **Both deliberate "show me the player" gestures raise `focusRequest`** (`openPlayer` and `restore`, consumed by a layout effect in `FloatingMediaPlayer`), because the widget is never unmounted and so has no mount to hang focus off: without it a keyboard-opened player is keyboard-DEAD until clicked, and Escape-to-minimize does nothing. Opening or queueing a FILE deliberately does not raise it - that would pull the caret out of whatever the user was typing in. It loads PAUSED at the remembered position - opening the widget is a request to SEE it, not to start listening - and it RE-QUEUES a target that only survives in history, because only queue entries are ever drawn, so activating a history-only id resolved a target, rendered nothing, and read as the feature being broken. That is also why `closeItem` keeps an item's `resumeTimes` entry when the item lands in history: reopening from "recently played" has to resume where the user stopped, and only a track leaving BOTH lists loses its bookmark. Classify media with `getOpenedMediaKind()` in `src/renderer/utils/mediaItems.ts`. **A `file://` link clicked in markdown must go through `openFileUrl()`** (`src/renderer/utils/openFileUrl.ts`), never straight to `shell.openPath`: the file-link plugins emit `file://` for any path outside the project root, so an agent mentioning `~/Scratch/x.mp3` was handing it to the OS player and bypassing the choke point entirely. It PERCENT-DECODES the path, which is not optional - a `file://` href is a URL, so both an agent writing one by hand and mdast-util-to-hast's `normalizeUri` encode it, and the literal `%20` handed to `fs.readFile` is an ENOENT that `handleMainPanelFileClick` reports by simply returning. Every media file in a folder with a space in its name was a link that did nothing when clicked. Same rule and same reason as `resolveLocalImagePath` in `Markdown/components/LocalImage.tsx`; do NOT strip the scheme without decoding at a new call site. **The player's height is derived, never stored** (`src/renderer/utils/mediaFloatGeometry.ts`): chrome for audio, chrome + `width / aspect` for video, where the aspect is the file's real `videoWidth / videoHeight` and the chrome is MEASURED (font metrics differ per platform, so a hard-coded transport height letterboxes video somewhere). Only position and a per-kind width persist, so a mixed queue reshapes itself as it advances. **The toast stack steps OVER the widget, never through it:** both open in the bottom-right corner and toasts render at `Z_LAYERS.TOAST` (100000) against the player's 60, so an arriving notification painted straight across the widget and read as the player having closed itself. `FloatingMediaPlayer` publishes its live rect as `floatFootprint` - expressed from the bottom and right edges, so the lane compares the two without needing a viewport measurement of its own - and `ToastContainer` raises itself by `mediaFloatLaneLift()`. Do NOT fix a collision like this by raising the player's z-index instead: toasts have to stay readable over modals, and a video frame would swallow them. **Minimize and close are different actions and must stay that way:** the `-` button sets `dismissed` (widget hidden, audio continues, `NowPlayingIndicator` in the Left Bar header becomes its play/pause via `requestToggle` plus a separate restore button), while `x` calls `closeItem` (player released, sound stops, rest of the queue intact). Minimize is a `hidden` STYLE FLAG on `FloatingMediaPlayer`, never a different render branch in `MediaPlaybackHost` - moving the media element in the React tree unmounts it, and that runs the spec's pause steps. **Escape minimizes, never closes**, and it is scoped to the widget (a `tabIndex={-1}` root plus `onKeyDown`) rather than registered with the layer stack: the player is not modal, it floats over a workspace the user keeps typing in, so Escape is only its to claim while focus is inside it. Wiring Escape to `closeItem` would let a reflex kill playback, which is the one thing the minimize/close split exists to prevent. It defers twice - to an open queue/history list, and to `document.fullscreenElement`, since Escape is already how a user leaves fullscreen - and `MediaViewer` swallows it only when its speed menu is open, so exactly one thing happens per press. **Neither list shows the loaded track:** history records DEPARTURES (a track enters when it stops being active) and `historyForActiveChange` also strips the incoming id, while the queue menu filters via `upcomingMediaItems()` at display time - the active item must stay in `items` or `stepMediaItem` loses prev/next. `clearQueue` keeps the loaded track, since clearing what is queued must not stop playback. **Queueing un-hides the widget** (`enqueueMedia` clears `dismissed` and `dormant`), and that is deliberate rather than a violation of the minimize invariant above: adding files is a request to SEE the queue, so doing it into a hidden player produces no visible response anywhere in the app and reads as the command having done nothing. It is safe because un-hiding is not interrupting - the loaded track is untouched and keeps playing. It fires even when NOTHING was added: queueing files that are all already queued adds zero items, and gating the reveal on `added > 0` made "Add to Play Queue" a silent no-op on exactly the second attempt, which is when a user retries a command that appeared to do nothing. For the same reason the idle branch loads the first file the user POINTED AT rather than the first one newly added, so re-queueing something the queue already holds still puts a widget on screen. A control that hides itself must not silently stop media, and a close button that only hid it leaves audio coming from nowhere. User docs: [media-player.md](../media-player.md).
- **Opening a clicked link:** `openUrl(url, { ctrlKey })` in `src/renderer/utils/openUrl.ts`, over `parseUrlRoutingHint()` / `isWebUrl()` / `stripUrlRoutingHint()` in `src/shared/urlRoutingHint.ts`. `openUrl` is the ONE place that decides Maestro browser tab vs system browser: the `useSystemBrowser` setting, inverted by Ctrl OR Cmd (pass `e.metaKey || e.ctrlKey` - on macOS Ctrl-click is the secondary click, so `ctrlKey` alone leaves the inversion unreachable), and overridden per link by a routing hint (`maestro+https://...` always a Maestro tab, `system+https://...` always the system browser, prefix stripped before opening). Every link surface gates on `isWebUrl()` rather than `/^https?:\/\//`, or a hinted link silently stops being a link there. A hint in front of anything but http(s) is `invalid` and opens NOTHING: the fallback for an unknown scheme is `shell.openExternal`, which would hand `maestro+file://` or `system+ssh://` to the OS. Do NOT call `window.maestro.shell.openExternal` from a link's click handler (`linkify.tsx` did, and ignored both the setting and the hint).
- **Right-click menu on an image:** already done, app-wide. `<ImageContextMenuHost>` (`src/renderer/components/ImageContextMenuHost.tsx`) is mounted once in `App.tsx` and runs ONE delegated `contextmenu` listener that resolves the image from the click target, so raster `<img>`, inline `<svg>`, Mermaid charts, thumbnails, and the lightbox all get Copy Image / Save to Project / Save As with **zero per-surface wiring**. Do NOT add an `onContextMenu` to a new image surface or hand-roll copy/save buttons - a new surface is covered the moment it renders. To opt a surface out (it owns its own right-click behavior), put `data-no-image-menu` on its container. Export helpers live in `src/renderer/utils/imageExport.ts` (`saveImageToProject()` writes to `.maestro/diagrams/` and works over SSH). See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Right-Click Image Menu](UI-PATTERNS.md#right-click-image-menu-imagecontextmenuhost).
- **Saving generated image bytes to disk:** `saveImageDataUrlToDisk(dataUrl, defaultName?)` in `src/renderer/utils/imageExport.ts`. The sibling `saveImageElementToDisk()` exports an image that is already in the DOM; this one takes bytes produced on the fly (a page screenshot, a canvas render) that have no element to point at. Both go through the native save dialog and then `fs.writeImageFile` - `fs.writeFile` is UTF-8 and would corrupt raster bytes - and both fall back to a browser download when the dialog is unavailable (web renderer). A cancelled dialog reports `{ saved: false }` with no `error`, so a caller must not treat every non-save as a failure toast. Unlike `saveImageToProject()` it does not call `requestFileTreeRefresh`, because the user usually points it outside any workspace.
- **Reading a pasted or dropped image (or any Blob) as a data URL:** `readBlobAsDataUrl(blob)`, `imageFilesIn(dataTransfer)`, `downscaleImageDataUrl(src, maxEdge)` in `src/renderer/utils/imageData.ts`. `imageFilesIn` takes a paste's `clipboardData` or a drop's `dataTransfer` and returns its image files in order (a paste lists them under `items`, a drop under `files` too). `downscaleImageDataUrl` draws a WebP copy no longer than `maxEdge` on its longer side and hands back the original when it is already small or the canvas cannot draw, so a caller never loses the image. The Quick Chat window stages images with the first two and its engine draws message previews with the third; the clipboard read and image export use `readBlobAsDataUrl` instead of their own `FileReader` promise.
- **Screenshotting a surface exactly as it is painted:** `window.maestro.shell.capturePage(rect?)` (preload in `src/main/preload/system.ts`, handler `window:capturePage` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/system.ts`). Reach for it instead of serializing a canvas or rasterizing DOM whenever the thing on screen is composed of several render paths - the Document Graph is a `<canvas>`, a React markdown preview pane, and an HTML legend, and only the compositor knows what the user is actually looking at. Three things the handler already solves, so do not re-solve them: it captures `event.sender` rather than the main window, so a surface opened in a second window shoots itself; it floors and clamps the rect, because `getBoundingClientRect()` returns floats and Chromium answers an out-of-range region with an empty image; and it resolves `null` rather than an empty data URL when there is nothing to capture. Callers must dismiss any modal of their own and wait two animation frames first, or the shot includes the dialog that asked for it. Feature-detect (`!!window.maestro?.shell?.capturePage`) before offering the affordance - the web renderer has no bridge.
- **Loading an image for canvas compositing:** `loadImageElement(src)` in `src/renderer/utils/loadImage.ts`. Wraps `new Image()` + `onload` / `onerror` in a promise and REJECTS on failure, so a caller can never draw a half-decoded element and report success. `compositeAnnotatedImage` and `cropImageDataUrl` both use it, which is what keeps a crop and the save that follows it from disagreeing about whether the source loaded. Accepts anything an `<img src>` accepts, including `maestro-image://` store refs, and the resolved element is decoded so `naturalWidth` / `naturalHeight` are safe to read immediately. Two other loaders in the renderer look like duplicates and are NOT - do not "consolidate" them without reading why: `loadImage()` in `AchievementShareButton.tsx` resolves `null` on purpose (a missing avatar or GitHub logo falls back to a drawn placeholder rather than failing the whole share card), and `svgToPngDataUrl()` in `imageExport.ts` sets `img.decoding = 'async'` before assigning `src`, a hint this helper does not expose. Reach for `loadImageElement` when a failed load must abort the operation.
- **Pan and zoom a diagram or image:** `usePanZoom({ minZoom?, maxZoom?, resetKey? })` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/usePanZoom.ts` owns the canvas math (wheel zoom about the cursor, drag pan, stepped zoom, `fitToView`); `openZoomViewer(element, title?)` in `src/renderer/components/ZoomViewer/zoomViewerStore.ts` opens the one app-wide full-window viewer (`ZoomViewerHost`, mounted in `App.tsx`) on an already-rendered `<svg>` or `<img>`. Before this, `FilePreview/ImageViewer` held the only copy of the math and nothing else could zoom. Invariants: the wheel listener is NATIVE and non-passive, because React's `onWheel` is passive and a trackpad pinch (ctrl-wheel) would fall through to Chromium's page zoom; the drag tracks on `window` so it survives leaving the canvas; view state is one `{zoom,x,y}` object so zoom-about-a-point is one updater, not a `setOffset` nested inside a `setZoom` updater. The viewer CLONES an SVG via `prepareSvgForViewer()` (drops fixed width/height, keeps or derives a viewBox) rather than re-rendering its source, and must not put `will-change: transform` on vector content, which would blur it. Surfaces add a way in with `<ExpandToViewerButton resolveTarget>` inside a `relative group` wrapper; the image right-click menu already offers Expand for everything else, so do not wire per-surface menus.
- **Diagram content clipped at the SVG edge:** `expandSvgViewBoxToContent(svg, padding?)` in `src/renderer/utils/svgViewBox.ts`, called by `MermaidRenderer` immediately after the diagram is appended. Most mermaid types size their viewBox from laid-out content (`setupGraphViewbox` pads a `getBBox()`), but a few do not: `quadrantChart` hard-codes `viewBox="0 0 500 500"` and then draws the title centered at x=250 and point labels wherever the data puts them, so a long title or an edge-of-quadrant label is painted outside the viewport and the browser clips it - the visible damage is a chart title with both ends sliced off. The fix is NOT `overflow: visible`, which would let the diagram paint over the surrounding chat text; the viewBox is widened to the union of its declared box and the real content bounds, and the size attributes are scaled by the same factor so the diagram keeps its on-screen scale instead of shrinking to fit the wider box. Three properties to preserve if you touch it: it must run AFTER the element is in the document, because `getBBox()` needs a layout; it pads only the sides that actually overflow, because padding every edge nudges a correctly-sized diagram into looking like an overflow and rescales it; and it returns false untouched when `getBBox` is missing or throws, which is what keeps it inert in jsdom and on a detached or `display:none` SVG.
- **The message of a rejected IPC call:** `ipcErrorMessage(error)` in `src/renderer/utils/ipcErrorMessage.ts`. A throw from a main-process handler reaches the renderer wrapped by Electron as `Error invoking remote method '<channel>': Error: <what the handler said>`. A surface that prints `error.message` raw shows the user the channel name and the error class, and buries the one sentence written for them. The helper drops the wrapper and the class name behind it (`Error`, `TypeError`, any other) and returns the handler's own message whole, colons included. A message that was never wrapped comes back unchanged, and so does one that only starts with `Error: ` by itself, because nothing says that text was plumbing. A value that is not an `Error` is shown as text. It replaced a private `errorText()` in `WebLoginUsers.tsx`, which split on `': Error: '` and kept the LAST piece: that cut a handler message that itself quoted an inner error down to its tail, and it left a `TypeError` wrapper in place. Two older call sites still strip the wrapper inline with a regex and keep the `Error: ` class prefix in the toast (`src/renderer/hooks/groupChat/useGroupChatHandlers.ts`, `src/renderer/services/remoteGroupChat.ts`). Move them here when you touch them. Do NOT add another local copy.

## Keyboard and shortcuts

- **Which bindings the live keyboard mode matches:** `resolveEffectiveShortcuts(base, overrides)` plus `overridesWithEdits()` and `isOverrideInherited()` in `src/shared/effectiveShortcuts.ts`, read through `selectActiveShortcuts()` / `selectActiveTabShortcuts()` / `selectVimDisplacedActions()` in `src/renderer/stores/settingsShortcutsSlice.ts` and the `useActiveShortcuts()` hook in `src/renderer/hooks/settings/useActiveShortcuts.ts`. There are now TWO independent persisted key sets (`shortcuts` / `tabShortcuts`, and the sparse `vimShortcuts` / `vimTabShortcuts` overrides) and exactly ONE is live, which is what lets Vim mode reuse a chord default mode gives to another action. Pairing a bucket with a mode by hand is silent in both directions: a matcher reading `shortcuts` while Vim mode is live fires chords the user cannot see in Settings while the ones they can see do nothing, and a writer reading one bucket and writing the other destroys the "each mode keeps its own customizations" guarantee. So **every keyboard path takes its maps from `useActiveShortcuts()`** (or the selectors outside React) and never from `useSettingsStore(s => s.shortcuts)`; `useKeyboardShortcutHelpers` is handed the resolved pair and knows nothing about modes. Three rules the resolver owns, each of which was a bug when it was missing: an override id is looked up in BOTH override buckets, PREFERRING the one that mirrors the base map declaring the action, because `VIM_TAB_SHORTCUTS` carries `prevTab` / `nextTab` while `DEFAULT_SHORTCUTS` declares them, and reading the tab bucket first would let the shipped chord shadow the user's own recording forever; an inherited chord that COLLIDES with an override chord (`shortcutKeysCollide`, so Ctrl and Cmd count as one modifier) is dropped to `keys: []` and its id reported in `displaced`, because two actions answering one keypress resolve by whichever branch of the keyboard handler runs first; and an override of `keys: []` is the user clearing the binding in THIS mode, distinct from an entry with no `keys` at all, which means inherit. The write side diffs the whole map the recorder hands back against the EFFECTIVE map it was shown, so one edited row writes one override - persisting the map wholesale would turn the entire inherited set into explicit overrides on the first edit and freeze that day's default chords. The selectors cache on the identity of the four buckets they read: zustand re-runs them on every settings change, and a fresh map each time re-renders every keyboard consumer in the app when a font size changes. The only mouse route between modes is the toggle at the top of `src/renderer/components/Settings/tabs/ShortcutsTab.tsx`, which is also the one surface that shows what a mode costs: an inherited row is tagged `Default`, and a `displaced` action is moved to a "Needs a <mode> binding" section at the top of the list rather than left sitting among the hundred rows below reading "Not set". Its conflict check scans the ACTIVE mode's effective pair plus `FIXED_SHORTCUTS` and nothing else (the other mode's chords are free, because the two sets are never live together) and compares with `shortcutKeysCollide`, not `shortcutKeysEqual`, or it hands out Ctrl+O while Cmd+O already fires something. Every surface that NAMES a mode (that toggle, the Quick Actions entry, the menu item, the toast on a switch) takes the word from `KEYBOARD_MODE_LABELS` in `src/shared/shortcut-types.ts` beside `KEYBOARD_MODES`; it is keyed by the union, so a third mode is a compile error in each of them instead of a mode that renders as its raw id in one place.
- **Matching a key sequence (`g g`, `g t`):** `createSequenceMatcher()` and `SEQUENCE_TIMEOUT_MS` in `src/renderer/utils/shortcutSequence.ts`. A chord is one event, so `eventMatchesShortcutKeys()` in `src/renderer/utils/shortcutMatch.ts` can answer it from the event alone; a sequence is several events in a row, and the only thing this module adds is the memory of the previous one. It adds NOTHING to what a match means: every step goes through `eventMatchesShortcutKeys`, so modifier equality, Shift-rewritten punctuation, Shift-rewritten digits and the Alt/`e.code` fallback behave identically in a sequence and in a chord, and stay symmetric with `buildKeysFromEvent()` in `src/renderer/utils/shortcutRecorder.ts`. A second copy of those rules here would drift until a sequence step accepted a key its own chord spelling rejected, which is the worst kind of keyboard bug because nothing on screen explains it. `feed(event, bindings)` returns one of three outcomes and the caller owes each a different thing: `'no-match'` falls through to the chord ladder untouched (the ONLY outcome that must not be treated as handled), `'consumed-prefix'` gets `preventDefault()` and a return, `'matched'` dispatches `actionId` and records the usage against that action id. `bindings` is passed per call (anything shaped `{ id, sequence? }`, so `Object.values(effectiveShortcuts)` works with no adapter) rather than captured at construction, so a settings write cannot rebuild the matcher and lose a half-typed prefix. Seven rules, each of which was a bug when it was missing: an event carrying Meta, Ctrl or Alt is never a step and clears the run, which is what leaves the whole existing chord surface untouched (and is why bare keys are bound through `sequence`, never `keys`, since a `keys` binding fires regardless of focus); Escape clears unconditionally and returns `'no-match'` so the layer stack keeps first claim on it; a step matching no live prefix clears the buffer, so an abandoned run cannot complete minutes later; a MODIFIER-ONLY keydown (`Shift`, `CapsLock`, ...) returns `'no-match'` and LEAVES the prefix alone, because Shift's own keydown precedes the character it shifts and treating it as a failed step makes every sequence with a capital in it untypeable; the timeout is LAZY, checked against the last capture when the buffer is next read rather than with a timer, so there is no DOM global, nothing to cancel on unmount and no callback that can fire into a dead component (a consumer that must repaint when the prefix dies, like the prefix indicator, owns its own timer on the same exported constant); ambiguity resolves SHORT, so a step that both completes one sequence and prefixes a longer one fires the completion immediately rather than waiting out a second of dead keyboard, which is exactly why a prefix relation is a CONFLICT at record time (`shortcutSequencesCollide`) instead of a runtime race; and two identical sequences resolve by binding order, the defined tie-break behind that conflict check. Normal mode is the CALLER's gate: the matcher has no DOM and no React and knows nothing about focus or modals, so the keyboard handler checks `isNormalMode()` and `hasOpenLayers()` before feeding it anything, which is what keeps it unit testable on plain objects. The candidate list is PINNED when the prefix starts, so a `bindings` array that changes mid-run cannot retarget a half-typed sequence at another action; end a run that stopped being meaningful (mode switch, window blur) with `reset()`. `prefix()` is the captured steps for display, identity-stable while unchanged and expired on read, spelled the way the BINDING spells them rather than reconstructed from the event, so the indicator renders through the same formatter as a chord and no inverse of `buildKeysFromEvent` is needed for native events. `collectSequenceBindings(...maps)` in the same file is how the list is built: hand it the LIVE mode's effective maps (the pair `isShortcut` and `isTabShortcut` are given, never the raw buckets, or sequences from the mode that is not live would match), and it returns one entry per action that carries a non-empty `sequence`, keyed by the MAP KEY because that key is the dispatch address the matched id is fed back through. It returns a fresh array, so memoize it on the maps' identity. The wiring lives in `src/renderer/hooks/keyboard/useMainKeyboardHandler.ts`, reached through the keyboard context (`ctx.sequenceBindings`) rather than through `useKeyboardShortcutHelpers`, which stays a pure per-event chord lookup shared with surfaces that match a single chord. Two things about that wiring are load bearing. The block sits BELOW every early return above the chord ladder (terminal focus recovery, the macOS Ctrl bypass, the layer guard, the sidebar / Enter / Escape navigation handlers) because each of those already owns bare keys for a better reason, and ABOVE the ladder itself so a bare step is not first matched as a chord. And a match does NOT get its own dispatch: `withForcedAction()` swaps in a view of the context whose three matchers answer about the matched action id, so the sequence runs that action's EXISTING ladder branch, guards and all. Never add a second action-id-to-handler table for sequences - an action wired into the ladder later would then work from its chord and silently do nothing from its sequence.
- **Showing a key sequence in progress:** `<SequencePrefixIndicator>` in `src/renderer/components/SequencePrefixIndicator.tsx`, fed through `publishSequencePrefix()` / `clearSequencePrefix()` / `useSequencePrefixStore` in `src/renderer/stores/sequencePrefixStore.ts`. A chord gives its own feedback - the action happens. A sequence gives none until it completes, so the first `g` of `g g` looks like a dead keyboard, and a user who mistypes the second key never learns the app was waiting on them. This chip is the whole of that feedback: one `<ShortcutKeys>` cluster per captured STEP (never one call with the whole prefix, which that component draws as alternatives - "esc or ⌘ W" - and `g g` is a run, not a choice), plus a trailing ellipsis for "still listening". The store exists because `createSequenceMatcher()` is pure by design and the one instance lives inside the keydown effect's closure in `useMainKeyboardHandler`, where no component can reach it. The handler publishes `sequenceMatcher.prefix()` VERBATIM, so the matcher stays the single authority on what the prefix is and the chip can never show a run the matcher has already dropped; four of the five ways a prefix dies (match, abort, Escape, window blur) arrive as an empty prefix with nothing for the caller to branch on. The fifth is the timeout, and it is the one the matcher cannot report: it expires LAZILY, on the next read, so a user who presses `g` and walks away never triggers it. The matcher's answer stays correct either way, but a painted chip does not, so the INDICATOR owns that timer, keyed on `capturedAt` and computing the time LEFT rather than scheduling a fresh `SEQUENCE_TIMEOUT_MS` - a re-render partway through a run must not extend it. `setSequencePrefix` skips a write that changes nothing, by identity first and then by "both empty", which is what makes it safe for the handler to publish on EVERY keydown: an unrelated keypress must not re-render the chip or restart its clock, and the matcher's shared empty array is not this store's, so the identity check alone does not catch a reset. Mounted once in `AppShell` beside the other app-wide hosts rather than in the composer's pill stack (`ThinkingStatusPill`, `ExecutionQueueIndicator`, `QuitWhenIdleIndicator`): those only exist while an agent session owns the main panel, and a sequence is typeable in a group chat, in the log viewer and on the empty state, which is exactly where someone is most likely to be navigating by keyboard. Bottom CENTER because toasts are configurable to any of the four corners, so the center is the one spot on the bottom edge nothing else can be dragged or configured into; it is `pointer-events-none`, lives at most a second, and a prefix can only be live while focus is outside every text field, so overlapping the composer costs the user nothing they were looking at. `data-shortcut-hint` goes on the ROOT, not just the caps inside it - the phone rule that retires every chord badge would otherwise leave an empty bubble on screen.
- **Switching the live keyboard mode:** `switchKeyboardMode(mode?)` in `src/renderer/services/keyboardMode.ts`, beside `nextKeyboardMode()`, `findKeyboardModeToggleShadow(shortcuts, tabShortcuts)` / `keyboardModeToggleShadow()`, and `KEYBOARD_MODE_TOGGLE_KEYS`. FOUR routes switch modes (the `Opt+Cmd+V` chord in `useMainKeyboardHandler.ts`, the Quick Actions entry in `featureCommands.ts`, the hamburger row in `HamburgerMenuContent.tsx`, and the Settings toggle), and all but the last call this service rather than `setKeyboardMode` directly. The reason is the acknowledgement: every chord in the app may have just changed under the user, and the toast is the only thing that says which set is now live - and when the switch is INTO Vim mode it is also the only unprompted place the `displaced` actions are named, which matters because a displaced action has no chord at all until the user records one. Four decisions the service owns. The toggle chord lives in `FIXED_SHORTCUTS`, not `DEFAULT_SHORTCUTS`, because it is the way back OUT of the mode you just switched into: a rebindable toggle could be rebound (or displaced) by the mode it switches to, leaving the user in a binding set whose exit key no longer exists. The USER'S binding wins anyway - `Opt+Cmd+V` was `toggleConcerto`'s default once (`SHORTCUT_DEFAULT_REMAPS`) and anyone may bind it deliberately, so the handler asks `keyboardModeToggleShadow()` first and stands down, letting the event fall through to that action's own branch; the Shortcuts tab then names the action that took it, because a documented key that silently does nothing is worse than no key. The shadow check compares with `shortcutKeysCollide` so a Windows `Ctrl+Alt+V` counts, and skips `keys: []` or every unbound action would read as a shadow. `nextKeyboardMode()` steps through `KEYBOARD_MODES` rather than flipping two values, so a third mode becomes a step in the cycle instead of a mode the keyboard can never reach. And a switch to the mode already live is a no-op with NO toast: an acknowledgement for a change that did not happen teaches the user the toast means nothing. The Settings toggle is the deliberate exception - it also has to disarm its own pending reset, and the list of rows below it IS the visible acknowledgement. Mode names come from `KEYBOARD_MODE_LABELS`; the resolver, the two buckets, and the Shortcuts tab are the **Which bindings the live keyboard mode matches** entry above.

- **Modifier-key display text:** `formatKey()`, `formatShortcutKeys()`, `formatMetaKey()` (symbol: `⌘` / `Ctrl`), `formatMetaKeyName()` (prose: `Command` / `Ctrl`), `formatAltKeyName()` (prose: `Option` / `Alt`) in `src/renderer/utils/shortcutFormatter.ts`. Do NOT hard-code `⌘`, `Cmd+`, `Ctrl+`, `Option`, or `Alt` in UI copy, tooltips, or setting descriptions - it renders the wrong key on the other platform. Outside the renderer (CLI, main process) use `formatShortcutKeysFor(keys, isMac)` / `formatKeyFor()` in `src/shared/shortcutKeys.ts`, which owns the key maps; the renderer file is just the platform binding over it.
- **Advertising a shortcut next to the control that fires it:** `ShortcutHint` (`variant="chip"` for an inline key-cap, `variant="row"` for a hint header pinned above a dropdown's option list) and `shortcutSuffix(keys)` (for a `title=` tooltip) in `src/renderer/components/ui/ShortcutHint.tsx`. Maestro is keyboard-first, so every clickable route to an action that ALSO has a chord should show that chord where the user is already looking; this is the one component that draws it. Four byte-identical local `ShortcutHint` definitions had accumulated across the tab-bar components before it was extracted. Two invariants: read the chord from the LIVE shortcut map (`useSettingsStore(s => s.shortcuts)` / `s.tabShortcuts`) rather than spelling a default combo into the string, or a user who rebinds sees our chord instead of theirs; and both helpers render NOTHING for an empty or missing `keys`, because an action can ship unbound and a blank key-cap advertises a combo that does nothing. When a row already has a right-aligned badge, wrap the badge and the key-cap in ONE `ml-auto` container - two auto margins on siblings split the free space and strand the badge mid-row. Both shapes draw the chord with `<ShortcutKeys>` (same file): one accent-tinted cap per key, `whitespace-nowrap shrink-0`, so a chord never stacks vertically in a narrow row or a wrapping tooltip. It is the ONE look a chord has as UI: use it directly wherever a chord is drawn without a ShortcutHint around it (`HoverTooltip`'s `shortcut` renders through it; pass raw keys `['Meta', 'k']`, or alternatives `[['Escape'], ['Meta', 'w']]`), pass `onAccent` on an accent-filled row, and never hand-roll a `<kbd>` or a `font-mono` badge for a chord. `shortcutText()` is the plain-text twin for a native `title=`. Tests find a drawn chord with `getShortcutKeys(keys)` from `src/__tests__/helpers/shortcutKeys.ts` (its text is split across caps, so `getByText(chord)` no longer matches).

  **Tab overlay-menu shortcut badge:** `<ShortcutHint keys={...} theme={theme} />` in `src/renderer/components/ui/ShortcutHint.tsx` - the same component the entry above describes, and the only copy. Every tab item's overlay menu (AI, file, terminal, browser, group) uses it; it was previously re-declared inline, byte-identical, in four components, and then a second time as a TabBar-local module. Do NOT re-declare it locally or hand-roll the `ml-auto` badge span.

- **Keyboard-shortcut hint / kbd pill:** `<Keycap>` and `<KeycapHint>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/Keycap.tsx`. Draws a key as a physical key (face, border, bottom lip) instead of a bare glyph in a caption. `pressed` sinks the cap so a surface can echo the real keypress - drive it from a short timer, never from `keyup`, because a held key repeats without ever sending one. `onClick` makes the hint the control itself, which is how a legend can double as the Apply / Cancel row and still satisfy the graphical-exit rule. Do NOT hand-roll another `<kbd>` pill. See [UI-PATTERNS.md -> Keycaps](UI-PATTERNS.md#keycaps-keycap--keycaphint).
- **A system-wide (OS-level) hotkey:** `setNamedGlobalHotkey(id, keys, onPress)` and `keysToAccelerator(keys)` in `src/main/global-hotkey-manager.ts`. Every hotkey that fires while Maestro is in the background goes through this one registry, keyed by owner (`show` for the summon hotkey via `setGlobalShowHotkey`, `quickChat` for Quick Chat). Each owner holds at most one accelerator and re-binding releases the old one, so a typo never leaves a stale combo grabbed system-wide. Two owners may NOT share a combo: the second registration returns `false` (Electron would otherwise silently route the key to whichever registered last). `keysToAccelerator` maps the recorder's literal space (`' '`, and the non-breaking space macOS reports for Option+Space) to `Space`; without that, recording Option+Space produced an accelerator Electron rejects. Do NOT call `globalShortcut.register` directly from a feature.
- **Whether a chord may be bound at all:** `findReservedShortcutCombo(keys)` over `RESERVED_SHORTCUT_COMBOS` in `src/shared/shortcutKeys.ts`. `Cmd/Ctrl+Shift+Arrow` is the OS's select-to-end inside a text field, and a Maestro binding on one wins in every input in the app, so the user loses the native behavior with no way to tell that apart from a broken text box. It is checked at BOTH ends and the second half is the one that is easy to skip: the recorder (`ShortcutsTab`) refuses a fresh binding, and `migrateShortcuts()` in `settingsStore.ts` strips one already on disk and falls back to the bundled default. A recorder-only guard leaves anyone who already bound the chord broken forever. The table is written in terms of `Meta` and comparison collapses `Ctrl`/`Command` into it, matching `eventMatchesShortcutKeys`, so one entry covers every platform - do NOT add a separate `Ctrl` row.
- **Whether a keystroke belongs to a text field (guard before `preventDefault`):** `isTextInputTarget(target)` and `isTextEntryTarget(target)` in `src/renderer/utils/messageScrollNavigation.ts`; `isEditingTextTarget(target)` / `isEditingTextFocused(doc?)` in `src/renderer/utils/editableTarget.ts`. A surface-level key handler that fires while the caret is in an input steals the keystroke from whatever the user is typing, and the bug reads as the app randomly misbehaving rather than as a shortcut conflict - a bare Enter in the markdown editor also opened the file tree's selected row, because the Files tab held the app's `activeFocus` while the DOM focus was elsewhere. `activeFocus` is APP state and DOM focus is the real thing; when they disagree, real focus wins. Pick by what the key is: **`isTextInputTarget`** counts every `<input>`, `<textarea>`, and contenteditable - use it for arrow keys and Enter, where a range slider legitimately owns Left/Right. **`isTextEntryTarget`** is narrower, counting only inputs that swallow typed characters, so a focused slider or checkbox cannot silently disable `Cmd+Z` or a single-letter tool key. It also counts anything with a `.cm-editor` or `.xterm` ancestor: CodeMirror keeps the caret in a contenteditable but a keydown can land on the scroller, a gutter, or a panel control, and xterm routes typing through a hidden helper textarea while painting the rest as plain divs and canvases, so in both the key is the user typing even when the literal target is a `<div>` that no `tagName` test would call a text field. That ancestor lookup runs against `Element` rather than `HTMLElement`, so an SVG icon inside a CodeMirror panel counts too, and it is the same `.xterm` test `useMainKeyboardHandler` had inline for terminal focus recovery - there is one spelling of it now. **`isEditingTextTarget`** asks the same question by instance rather than `tagName` (so it sees an `<input>` from another document/realm), and `isEditingTextFocused()` asks it of `document.activeElement` for handlers bound to `window` where the event target is `<body>`. Do NOT hand-roll another `tagName === 'INPUT' || ... || isContentEditable` chain: there are already ~8 inline copies across the renderer (`useKeyboardNavigation`, `DocumentGraphView`, `usePipelineKeyboard`, `MediaViewer`, and others) and they have drifted - some miss contenteditable, `MediaViewer`'s misses `<textarea>` entirely. Import one of these instead; see [DEDUP-TRACKER.md](DEDUP-TRACKER.md).

- **Whether a keystroke is free for a binding to claim (normal mode):** `isNormalMode(event, doc?)` in `src/renderer/utils/messageScrollNavigation.ts`, beside the `isTextEntryTarget()` it is built from. Vim's normal mode in Maestro is NOT a state machine and must never become one: it is exactly "nothing that accepts typed text holds this keystroke", asked of the DOM at the moment the key arrives. A mode flag would need an enter and an exit for every path focus can take - clicking the composer, a modal autofocusing its first input, CodeMirror mounting, xterm taking focus back after a tab switch, a window blur - and the first path anyone forgot would leave the flag reading "normal" while the user typed prose, so bare-key bindings would eat their characters with nothing on screen to explain why. Focus already IS that state and the DOM already tracks it correctly, so this asks the DOM and cannot desync from it. BOTH `event.target` and `document.activeElement` have to be free, because they disagree in both directions: a handler bound to `window` sees `<body>` as the target when focus sits on the body, and the image annotator deliberately leaves a composer textarea focused underneath its own overlay (`src/renderer/components/ImageAnnotator/annotatorKeyboard.ts`). Requiring both is the conservative side of that asymmetry - a wrongly rejected keystroke costs one Vim motion, while a wrongly accepted one steals a character out of something the user is writing. It answers the FOCUS half of normal mode and only that half: "no modal layer is open" lives in the layer stack, which is React context rather than DOM, so it stays with the caller - `useMainKeyboardHandler` checks `ctx.hasOpenLayers()` beside this before feeding an event to `createSequenceMatcher()`. The optional `doc` argument exists so a test can pin `activeElement` without driving real focus.
- **Hiding a switched-off Encore Feature's shortcut, menu item, or setting:** `availableShortcuts(map, encoreFeatures)` / `isShortcutAvailable(id, encoreFeatures)` over `SHORTCUT_ENCORE_FLAGS` in `src/renderer/utils/encoreShortcuts.ts`; `requiresEncore` on a `SearchableSetting` (`src/renderer/components/Settings/searchableSettings.ts`); `encoreFlag` on a `MenuCommand` in `src/main/app-menu.ts`, fed by `setMenuEncoreFeatures()`. A disabled feature must leave no trace, and gating only its hotkey handler, palette entry and hamburger item left three listings behind: Shortcuts Help and Settings -> Shortcuts still showed (and let the user rebind) "Maestro Cue" or "Computer History", the Usage Dashboard listed them as unused shortcuts and counted them in the mastery denominator (a chord that cannot fire holds the user below 100% forever), the macOS View menu kept a dead "Usage Dashboard" item, and Settings search returned a disabled feature's options. A new Encore Feature that adds a shortcut adds its id to `SHORTCUT_ENCORE_FLAGS`; one that adds a native menu item sets `encoreFlag`; one that adds a setting outside its own tile sets `requiresEncore` AND gates the rendered control on the same flag. Do NOT set `requiresEncore` on the feature's tile entry: the tile is how the feature gets switched on, so it has to stay findable. The recorder's conflict check in `ShortcutsTab` deliberately scans the UNFILTERED map, so a hidden chord cannot be handed to another action and collide when the feature comes back.
- **Hiding a switched-off Encore Feature's prompts and template variables:** `isPromptAvailable(promptId, encoreFeatures)` over the `encoreFlag` field of a `PromptDefinition` in `src/shared/promptDefinitions.ts`; `availableTemplateVariables(variables, encoreFeatures)` in `src/shared/templateVariables.ts`, read in a component through `useAvailableTemplateVariables(list?)` (or `getAvailableTemplateVariables()` at the moment a popup opens) in `src/renderer/hooks/input/useAvailableTemplateVariables.ts`. Settings -> Maestro Prompts listed `pianola-system`, `computer-history-system` and `_maestro-cue` for features that were off, and every `{{` picker offered `{{COMPUTER_HISTORY_DIR}}`, `{{MAESTRO_RUN_PATH}}` and the `{{CUE_*}}` family. Both filters are for LISTS only: a hidden prompt still loads for agents and `maestro-cli prompts get`, and a hidden variable still substitutes (to an empty string), so nothing a user already wrote breaks when a feature goes off. A variable declares `encoreFlag`; a `cueOnly` one belongs to `maestroCue` without saying so. Do NOT iterate `TEMPLATE_VARIABLES` in JSX directly. The stronger rule sits one level up: text that tells an agent to USE an Encore Feature never goes in `maestro-system-prompt.md`. It goes in its own prompt, delivered through `systemPromptSection` on the feature's `FirstPartyPluginDefinition` (`systemPromptSectionsFor()` in `src/shared/maestroSystemPrompt.ts`), which is what stopped every agent being told to answer on the Concerto stage while Concerto was off and its payloads were dropped.
- **How much of the keyboard the user has mastered:** `collectBoundShortcuts(...maps)` (denominator) and `countUsedBoundShortcuts(bound, usedIds)` (numerator) in `src/renderer/constants/keyboardMastery.ts`. Every mastery figure - the help modal's bar, the Usage Dashboard ring and its Unused Shortcuts list, the leaderboard payload, and the level-up check in `settingsStore` - runs the LIVE maps (`shortcuts`, `tabShortcuts`, `FIXED_SHORTCUTS`) through these, so they cannot disagree about the total. **A shortcut with no chord bound is excluded from both ends**: it can never be fired, so counting one in the denominator makes Keyboard Maestro unreachable forever, and listing one as "unused" tells the user to go press a chord that does not exist. The numerator is not `usedShortcuts.length` either - that list keeps ids whose binding was later cleared, which reports over 100%. Do NOT hand-roll another `Object.keys(DEFAULT_SHORTCUTS).length + ...` total; there were already two rival copies that disagreed with the surfaces rendering the number.
- **Arrow-key navigation over a list or grid:** `useListNavigation()` in `src/renderer/hooks/keyboard/useListNavigation.ts`. Pass `columns` for a 2-D grid (left/right step one item, up/down jump a row); grid mode keys on the option's PRESENCE, so a grid reflowed to one column still answers all four arrows. Omit it for a plain list, where left/right stay inert. For a responsive grid feed it `useGridColumnCount(el, itemCount)` from `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useGridColumnCount.ts`, which takes the ELEMENT (held in state via a callback ref, since a ref mutating in place is invisible to React and would keep measuring an unmounted grid) and reads the RESOLVED `grid-template-columns` - a hard-coded row width walks to the wrong tile as soon as an `auto-fill` grid reflows. Render the result as a roving tabindex over native `<button>`s. See [UI-PATTERNS.md](UI-PATTERNS.md#arrow-navigation-over-a-list-or-grid-uselistnavigation).
- **Bare `+` / `-` / `0` zoom on a surface:** `useScaleShortcuts(control, { enabled })` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useScaleShortcuts.ts`, over any `useScalePreference` control, with the keys named in the buttons' tooltips via `<ScaleControl shortcutHint>`. It is deliberately MODIFIER-FREE and skips any event carrying Cmd/Ctrl/Alt, because `Cmd+=` / `Cmd+-` is the app-wide font zoom and must keep working while a zoomable surface is open. It listens on `window` at CAPTURE rather than on the surface's node: focus falls to the body whenever a nested overlay closes, so a node-scoped handler goes dead exactly when the user comes back to the surface, and `stopPropagation` is what keeps a bare `0` or `-` out of the global shortcut handler. **Gate it with `enabled`, normally `useIsTopLayer(priority)`** - two stacked surfaces both answering the same unmodified key is the failure this replaces. `FilePreview` keeps its own inline copy of the same branch because that one sits inside a single ordered key chain where position decides which branch wins; every other surface rides the hook.
- **Keyboard navigation in a `<DualPaneFileEditor>` list:** built in. Up/Down walk the VISIBLE rows (collapsed categories are skipped, the ends do not wrap), and Backspace/Delete raise `onDeleteItem(selectedId)` - the list reports the intent, the consumer owns the confirmation. Both keys are ignored unless the event came from a row, so the "+ New" button in the same container cannot delete anything. Pass `autoFocusList` when the surface's whole job is walking the list (the Memory Viewer) so the keys work without clicking a row first; it fires on the FIRST selection rather than on mount (the list loads async) and defers to anything that already holds focus, so a user who went straight for the filter box keeps the caret. After a consumer-driven delete, bump `listFocusToken`: the focused row was just unmounted, focus falls to `<body>`, and the next Backspace silently does nothing, which reads as the keyboard dying halfway through a cleanup pass.

## UI components and widgets

- **A spinning loading icon:** `<Spinner>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/Spinner.tsx` (`icon` for a glyph other than `Loader2`, `decorative` when nearby text already says it). It rotates a wrapping `<span>`, never the `<svg>`: Chromium cannot composite a transform animation on an `<svg>` element, so `animate-spin` on a Lucide icon forces a main-thread style + paint pass on every frame for as long as it is on screen (measured ~120 main frames/s from one spinner, zero when wrapped). A field trace caught one such spinner keeping the renderer at full frame rate for the whole capture. Do NOT put `animate-spin` on an icon directly; a spinner that is on screen only for a short refresh is tolerable, a busy indicator that can stay up for minutes is not.
- **Tiny uppercase state chip:** `<MiniBadge>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/MiniBadge.tsx` - "WT" beside a worktree agent, "Snoozed" beside a tab. The generic text counterpart to `<CountBadge>` (a number and nothing else) and to the fixed-word domain pills beside it (`WorktreePill`, `GitRunningBadge`). Do NOT hand-roll another `text-[9px] px-1 rounded uppercase` span: the same chip was written per surface and drifts on padding and weight. The label is its own accessible name, so pass a real word rather than an abbreviation, unless the abbreviation is the established UI term (then pass `title` with the long form).
- **A labeled action button in a panel header:** `<HeaderActionButton>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/HeaderActionButton.tsx` - the "+ New Memory" / "+ New Session" / "Resume" pill. It was hand-rolled in five files as the same `px-3 py-1.5 rounded-lg text-sm font-medium` string. It sizes ONE STEP BELOW the header title (`text-xs`, icon `w-3.5`), deliberately: the copies used `text-sm`, which is exactly the title's size, so the button read as heavy as the heading it sits beside and dominated a header it is subordinate to. Pass the icon without size classes - the component sizes it. Do NOT hand-roll another accent pill, and do not "fix" it back to `text-sm`.
- **A text box that narrows a list:** `<FilterInput>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/FilterInput.tsx` - search icon, borderless input, optional `resultLabel` count, and a clear button that appears only once there is something to clear. Do NOT hand-roll another `<Search/> + <input>` pill; there are already a dozen near-identical copies that disagree on whether a clear button exists and what Escape does. Distinct from a FIND bar (`AutoRunSearchBar`, `TerminalSearchBar`), which walks matches inside one document and owns next/prev plus a match index - pick by question ("which rows do I see?" vs "take me to the next hit") and do not add match navigation to `<FilterInput>`. **Escape needs the host's help inside a modal:** the layer stack handles the key at capture on `window`, so the input never sees it and the surface closes instead of the filter clearing. Clear the query from the host's own `onEscape` first (see `MemoryViewer`). **Do NOT add a collapsed/magnifier-only mode to it** - that was tried and removed: hiding a pane's primary control to buy space in a crowded toolbar trades a layout problem for a discoverability one. When a toolbar cannot fit on one line, move what is NOT a control off that line (the Memory Viewer's corpus stats went to a footer).
- **Segmented toolbar (sort/filter pill bar):** `<SegmentedControl>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/SegmentedControl.tsx`. The "Sort by: [Name][Created][Queries]" bar above a grid or chart. It owns active-segment coloring, seam borders, `role="radiogroup"` semantics, arrow-key navigation, and a single tab stop; each segment gets `data-testid="${testId}-${value}"`. Do NOT hand-roll another `.map()` over buttons with `borderLeft` seams. A bar wider than its row SCROLLS sideways rather than clipping: the container was `overflow-hidden` (which is what rounds the corners), so a five-segment bar on a phone lost its last two options outright - invisible AND unclickable, with no scrollbar to hint they were there. Keep `overflow-y-hidden` beside the `overflow-x-auto` (alone, `overflow-y` computes to `auto` and adds a vertical bar) and keep the segments `shrink-0`, since a squeezed segment reads as a different label rather than a narrower one - shrinking stays the host's call via `shortLabel`. Distinct from `<RadioGroup>`, which is the stacked list-row form for settings panes - pick by layout, and do not add a `variant` prop to either to cover the other. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Segmented Toolbars](UI-PATTERNS.md#segmented-toolbars-segmentedcontrol).
- **Paginating a list already in memory:** `usePagination(items, pageSize, resetKey)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/usePagination.ts`, pure helpers in `src/renderer/utils/pagination.ts`, UI in `<Pager>` (`src/renderer/components/ui/Pager.tsx`). Do NOT confuse it with `useHistoryPagination`, which is the async IPC-backed windowing engine for data that arrives page by page - this one is for a list you already hold. The hook clamps the current page on every read rather than in an effect, which is what stops a filter that shrinks 1236 rows to 18 from rendering an empty grid under "Page 30 of 1"; pass a `resetKey` built from whatever the user can change that reorders the list (sort mode, filter mode, query text) so re-sorting does not strand them on an arbitrary slice. Render `<Pager>` in the TOOLBAR ROW, not under the list: a pager below a long grid inside a scrolling modal makes the user scroll to the bottom, click, then scroll back up.
- **Sortable table header:** `<SortableTh>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/SortableTh.tsx` + `useTableSort()` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useTableSort.ts`. The hook owns the flip-vs-switch rule (clicking the ACTIVE column reverses it; clicking a DIFFERENT column jumps to that column's own default direction instead of inheriting the previous one - inheriting is what makes a freshly-picked column show Z-A or least-used-first and read as broken data). The component owns `aria-sort` placement (on the `<th>`, not the control), a stable caret slot so switching columns doesn't reflow the header row, and a real `<button>` for the click target. Do NOT hand-roll `<th role="button" onClick>`: `role` grants the semantics without the tab stop or Enter/Space handling, so it announces as a button and then does nothing from the keyboard.
- **A list of editable `key = value` pairs:** `<KeyValueRows>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/KeyValueRows.tsx`, with `recordToKeyValueRows()` / `keyValueRowsToRecords()` beside it. The plain form of the control: a row per pair, a monospace input on each side, an optional eye that parks the row, a trash button. An SSH remote's environment variables and its extra `ssh -o` options are the same widget with different labels, and the second was about to become a copy of the first. Rows carry a stable `id` rather than being keyed by their key text - a list keyed by the editable field remounts the input on every keystroke and the caret jumps out after one character. `keyValueRowsToRecords` returns BOTH halves together (each `undefined` rather than `{}` when nothing survives, so a section the user emptied stores no key at all): a caller that wrote only the half it remembered would silently drop the other, which for the parked half means losing exactly the values parking exists to keep. The eye is opt-in via `onToggleRow` - without somewhere to keep a parked value there is nothing the button could do, so it is hidden rather than rendered dead. Only LIVE rows are validated by the consumer, because a value that cannot work right now is precisely what gets parked and a parked row that blocked Save would defeat the control. Distinct from `Settings/EnvVarsEditor`, which is the SHELL environment editor and owns secret masking, known-auth path completion, and absolute-path validation - pick by whether those behaviours are wanted, and do not add a mode to either to cover the other.
- **Switching a `key = value` entry off without deleting it:** `splitParkedEntries()` / `setKeysParked()` / `parkedPairKeys()` in `src/shared/parkedRecords.ts`. TWO records, never one record with an `enabled` flag. Being present in the ACTIVE record is exactly the same statement as being live, so every consumer - `resolveSshOptions`, the four SSH spawn sites, `resolveAgentEnvironment` - keeps reading one record and needs no filter; a single flag inside one record would put that filter in every read site, and the first one that forgets it ships the `ProxyCommand` the user thought they had switched off. The rule the module enforces so no caller has to: a key lives in exactly ONE of the two records, and a live row wins the key outright, because a key in both is unreachable state (re-enabling the parked copy after deleting the live one silently restores a value the user cannot see). `setKeysParked` IGNORES a key present in neither record rather than inventing it, so disabling something already deleted is a no-op instead of a resurrection. Three things ride it: the eye in `<KeyValueRows>`, the save handler, and the CLI's `--disable-*` / `--enable-*` flags, where disable runs BEFORE enable so one command can swap which of two keys is live. A `--clear-*` verb must wipe BOTH records - "remove all" that left parked entries behind leaves invisible state a later `--enable-*` could resurrect. Field pairs today: `shellEnvVars`/`shellEnvVarsDisabled`, `SshRemoteConfig.sshOptions`/`sshOptionsDisabled`, `SshRemoteConfig.remoteEnv`/`remoteEnvDisabled`.
- **Record view for one table row:** `<RecordDetailModal>` in `src/renderer/components/ui/RecordDetailModal.tsx` - the field/value flip of a row that every tabular preview opens on double-click beside a mouse and on a SINGLE tap under a coarse pointer (CSV/TSV via `CsvRowDetailModal`, parquet via `ParquetViewer`) - a finger cannot double-tap, iOS reserves the gesture, and a phone has no Enter key to fall back on. Callers pass a flat `fields` list plus their own `priority` / `resizeKey` / `testIdPrefix`; the mapping from a row to fields belongs in the caller, since a CSV row is positional strings and a parquet row is typed values. Do NOT hand-roll a second one - focus starts on the FIELD LIST rather than the filter input, because Left/Right step rows and Up/Down scroll and none of that works while a text input owns the caret (`/` moves focus to the filter).
- **Usage Dashboard card tile:** `<EntityTile>` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/EntityTile.tsx` - the shared tile behind every dashboard card grid (agent grid, per-tab grid): status dot, title, badges, corner age, subtitle, labeled stat row, sparkline, plus all four border states and the staggered `card-enter` animation. A new grid shapes data into `EntityTileStat[]`; it does not re-derive the chrome. Not the same thing as the widget library's `StatCard` (one headline metric), and it stays under `UsageDashboard/` because widgets may not import from there.
- **Usage Dashboard metric tile:** `<MetricCard>` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/MetricCard.tsx` - icon, uppercase caption, one headline figure, optional sub-line, with the `role="group"` + `aria-label` that lets a test assert on a tile by its label rather than by DOM position. The Auto Run, Resilience, and Wizard sections all render rows of these; the identical component had been pasted into the first two before it lived here. Pass PRE-FORMATTED strings (`formatNumber`, `formatDurationHuman`) - it does no math. Distinct from `<EntityTile>`, which is the richer card for an agent or group in a grid, and from the widget library's `StatCard`, which lives outside `UsageDashboard/` and cannot import from it.
- **Color for the Nth chart series:** `getSeriesColor(index, theme, colorBlindMode?)` in `src/renderer/components/UsageDashboard/chartUtils.ts`. Four byte-identical private copies (`getAgentColor` in `SessionStats`, `AgentEfficiencyChart`, `agentComparisonUtils`, `getProviderColor` in `ProviderTrendsChart`) were folded into it, so the same index is the same color when two charts sit side by side. `AgentUsageChart` keeps its own theme-less palette on purpose.
- **A label that must not truncate:** `useOptionalLabelFits(rowRef)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useOptionalLabelFits.ts` - returns whether an optional inline label still fits, so a caller renders it whole or omits it rather than clipping it. Reach for it when a fragment is worse than nothing: "GROUP CHA..." occupies the same row space as the full label and says less than the icon next to it. **Do NOT solve this with a container query**, which is what the Group Chats header tried first and why it clipped: the `fontSize` setting is written to `documentElement.style.fontSize`, so every rem-based size in a header scales with it while the panel's width stays in pixels, making any px threshold correct at exactly one font size; and conditional controls beside the label (a count badge, a sort button) change how much room is left, which a width threshold cannot see. The layout contract is load-bearing - the label needs `shrink-0` and the row needs `overflow-hidden`, so `scrollWidth` is the width the row wants and `clientWidth` is what it has, and overflow IS the "would have truncated" condition. The restore is the half that bites: once the label is gone the row no longer overflows, so re-showing on the same test oscillates; the hook remembers the width measured while the label was shown and returns it only at or above that, which cannot flap because that width demonstrably fit. Returns `true` before the first measurement and in jsdom, so a label renders by default. Distinct from `useElementWidth`, which reports a number for JS-computed layout and makes no decision.
- **Adding a control to the Left Bar header:** the row is three zones - identity (wand + wordmark, `shrink-0`), indicators (`flex-1 justify-center min-w-0`, `data-testid="sidebar-header-indicators"`), menu (`shrink-0`). A status control - the trophy badge, the now-playing pill, the LIVE toggle, whatever comes next - goes in the CENTER band, never beside the wordmark; `flex-1` is what centers it between the two fixed zones. The row neither wraps nor scrolls and the sidebar goes down to 256px, so it has a declared yield order rather than whatever CSS shrinks first. The MAESTRO wordmark is drawn IN FULL or dropped entirely (`showWordmark` in `SessionList.tsx`); it must never `truncate`, because a brand rendered as "MAE..." reads as a rendering bug. The now-playing pill is the shrink target of last resort - its filename is the one thing here that can be clipped without looking broken, so its label track is `minmax(0, max-content)` while the pill itself keeps its automatic minimum (never `min-w-0 overflow-hidden`, which clipped the restore button in half) - the transport never yields. Rem-built reserves (badge, pill) scale by `useHeaderTextDelta().remScale`. Reserve width for the form a control is CURRENTLY in (the pill sheds its filename when narrow, so its compact and labeled reserves are separate constants), and ask `selectNowPlayingVisible` whether the pill is on screen rather than re-deriving it. A reserve is charged only against what is still DRAWN: the LIVE label adds `headerBadgeWidth` only while `showWordmark` is true, because once the wordmark drops out the ~110px it vacated more than covers the badge, and charging anyway left a 256px sidebar showing a bare radio dot with empty space beside it. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → Left Bar Header Width Gates](UI-PATTERNS.md#left-bar-header-width-gates).
- **Color math and contrast:** `readableTextOn()`, `isReadableOn()`, `contrastRatio()`, `relativeLuminance()`, `blendColors()`, `transparentize()`, `adjustBrightness()`, `hexToRgb()` in `src/shared/colorContrast.ts`. Whenever a foreground and its background are BOTH derived from theme colors (diagram fills, chart labels, badges, generated SVG), run the foreground through `readableTextOn(preferred, backgrounds)` so a theme whose accent sits near its text color can't paint near-identical colors on top of each other. It returns the preferred color untouched when it already clears WCAG AA and nudges the theme's own color otherwise, so nothing snaps to hard-coded black/white.
- **Diagram labels on fills the AUTHOR chose:** `repairDiagramLabelContrast(svg)` in `src/renderer/utils/diagramLabelContrast.ts`, over `cssColorToHex()` in `src/shared/colorContrast.ts`. `initMermaid` derives label colors against fills it knows; an agent writing `style S4 fill:#f9e79f` (or a `classDef` fill) with no `color:` left the dark theme's near-white label on a pale yellow box. The repair runs on the LIVE DOM because `classDef` fills only resolve through `getComputedStyle`, measures each `g.node` / `g.cluster` shape fill against each text element in its own label, and rewrites failures with inline `!important` (mermaid's own label rules are `!important`) via `readableTextOn`, so passing pairs are untouched. `cssColorToHex` returns null for translucent, named, `hsl()`, and `url()` colors, which the repair treats as unmeasurable and skips. Any new surface that renders mermaid must call it after the SVG is attached.
- **Naming the OS file manager in copy:** `getFileManagerName(platform)` / `fileManagerName()` in `src/renderer/utils/platformUtils.ts` return the bare noun (`Finder` / `Explorer` / `File Manager`), and `getRevealLabel()` / `getOpenInLabel()` are built from it. Do NOT hard-code `Finder` in a hint, tooltip, or menu label - it reads wrong on Windows, and the failure is invisible on a Mac dev machine. Same class of bug as hard-coding `⌘`.
- **Rendering raw terminal output (ANSI):** `useAnsiConverter(theme)` in `src/renderer/hooks/ui/useAnsiConverter.ts` for the theme-aware `ansi-to-html` converter, then `getCachedAnsiHtml(text, theme.id, converter)` in `src/renderer/utils/textProcessing.ts` to convert, sanitize (DOMPurify), and cache. The 16 ANSI slots map onto the ACTIVE theme rather than the xterm palette, so do NOT hand-roll another `new Convert({...})` - a second palette drifts the first time a theme adds a color. Collapse carriage returns with `processCarriageReturns()` BEFORE converting, or a progress line arrives as a screen of dead rows, and run any regex over the output through `stripAnsiCodes()` first. Both of those live in `src/shared/stringUtils.ts` (the renderer module re-exports `processCarriageReturns` for convenience), so the MAIN process can reach them without pulling DOMPurify into a process with no DOM - `plainGitErrorText()` in `src/main/ipc/handlers/git/streaming.ts` uses them to turn a colored `--progress` failure into text a toast can show. That surface split is the whole point: the streaming runner ASKS for color, because the command console renders ANSI, but the `error` field it returns goes to a failure toast and a modal footer, which are plain `<div>`s - so a pre-push hook's colored output arrived there with a literal `[33m` in front of every word, and the `--progress` counter arrived with every intermediate percentage concatenated into one line. The renderer only owns half of this: **nothing Maestro spawns is a TTY**, so the producing side must force color too (`-c color.ui=always` for git, `FORCE_COLOR=1` / `CLICOLOR_FORCE=1` for whatever its hooks run) or the console renders a perfect wall of gray.
- **Previewing a parquet file:** `<ParquetViewer>` in `src/renderer/components/ParquetViewer/`, over the main-process engine in `src/main/parquet/` and the shared filter language in `src/shared/parquet/`. Parquet is the one previewable format that never crosses IPC: `fs:readFile` short-circuits it to a marker (`shared/parquet/preview.ts`) and the viewer queries windows of rows through `window.maestro.parquet.*`. Three invariants: **filtering and sorting round-trip to the engine** (filtering the loaded page would only ever search the first few hundred rows of a hundred million); **`matchedRows` is a LOWER BOUND until `complete` is true**, rendered `1,204+`, converged by a background `countAll: true` pass that doubles as the prefetch; and **hiding a column changes the projection**, so the engine stops decoding it. The one thing that silently loses data: **filter literals compile into the CONVERTED domain, not the physical one** - hyparquet converts footer statistics while parsing, so a `TIMESTAMP(MICROS)` bound is epoch MILLISECONDS, and compiling microseconds prunes every row group in the file while looking healthy. See [REMAINING-SYSTEMS.md → Parquet Query Engine](REMAINING-SYSTEMS.md#5-parquet-query-engine-srcmainparquet-srcsharedparquet).
- **Graphing a set of documents rather than one:** `BuildOptions.scopeFiles` / `scopeDirectory` in `src/renderer/components/DocumentGraph/graphDataBuilder.ts`, opened via `openGraphScope()` in `fileExplorerStore.ts`. Focus mode ("what does this document reach?") only creates nodes for files its BFS walks INTO, so a file nothing links to can never appear; scope mode ("how do these documents relate?") makes a node for every file in the set. Unreachable documents were being dropped TWICE - the builder's BFS, and then again by `prepareLayoutInput()` in `mindMapLayouts.ts`, which all six layout algorithms funnel through - so fixing only the builder renders nothing. They ride a `showOrphans` flag into the layout and get their own band via `positionOrphanNodesBottom()`, NOT a borrowed depth ring: an unreachable node has no depth, and giving it one draws it as a child of a center it has no link to. Scope mode also skips `startBacklinkScan`, which would pull in files outside the scope - the exact files the user did not select.
- **Sizing a Document Graph node:** `calculateNodeWidth(label, previewCharLimit)` and `calculateNodeHeight(previewText, previewCharLimit)` in `src/renderer/components/DocumentGraph/mindMapLayouts.ts`. Do NOT space a layout off the `NODE_WIDTH` constant: with previews off a document draws as a filename pill about a third of a card wide and a fraction of its height, so reserving the card width claims roughly five times the real footprint. That is what made the radial rings thousands of pixels across, and it reached the force layout too, whose `forceCollide` radius read the same constant. The layout vocabulary itself (`MindMapLayoutType`, `LAYOUT_LABELS`, `MIND_MAP_LAYOUT_TYPES`, `nextMindMapLayout`, `isMindMapLayoutType`) lives in `DocumentGraph/layoutTypes.ts`, a leaf module with no d3 import so `settingsStore` and the `Session` interface can name a layout without pulling in the engine - it had been written out three times, and a layout added to the graph was silently rejected on the way to disk by a copy nobody updated.
- **Filtering the Git Log:** `gitLogSearchTerms(query)` and `matchesGitLogTerms(commit, terms)` in `src/renderer/utils/gitLogSearch.ts`, plus `contractGitGraphNodes(nodes, keep)` in `src/renderer/utils/gitGraphLayout.ts`. The List and Graph views are built from DIFFERENT `git log` invocations (the list is the current branch, the graph is `--all`), so the one thing they must agree on is what counts as a hit - a query that hides a commit from one view and leaves it lit in the other reads as the filter being unreliable. Matching is SUBSTRING, never fuzzy: a commit hash is the motivating query, and a fuzzy matcher scores `a1b2c3d` against nearly every hash in the repository, so the one search where the user knows exactly what they want would return the whole log. Terms are ANDed on whitespace because `fix usage` has to find `fix(usage): ...`, which no single literal substring does; the fields are joined with a NEWLINE before the substring test, since concatenating them lets a term straddle two and match text that is nowhere on screen. Highlighting takes the same term ARRAY (`highlightMatches` accepts `string | string[]`), so what is marked is exactly what was matched on. **A filtered graph must be CONTRACTED, not merely thinned:** `contractGitGraphNodes` reattaches every survivor to the nearest kept ANCESTOR of each of its parents, preserving parent ORDER because `parents[0]` decides which branch a commit stays on and a second parent is what draws a merge. Simply removing nodes dangles every link through a dropped commit, so `assignGitGraphBranches` inherits nothing, hands each survivor a fresh anonymous lane, and the graph degenerates into a column of unconnected dots - strictly less information than the list beside it. Note the whole filter runs over the FETCHED window (`COMMIT_FETCH_LIMIT`, 200 commits), not the repository, so a hash older than that window will not be found; deepening that is a server-side `git log --grep` search, not a change to this predicate.
- **Layout and navigation for the Git Log graph:** `buildGitGraphCore()`, `computeGitGraphGeometry()`, `stepGitGraphVertical()`, `stepGitGraphHorizontal()`, `jumpGitGraphVertical()`, `gitGraphColumnEdge()`, `gitGraphTopCommit()`, `buildGitGraphTemplate()`, `assignGitGraphBranches()`, `pickBranchFromRefs()` in `src/renderer/utils/gitGraphLayout.ts`. `GitGraphView` DRAWS the graph and `GitLogViewer` NAVIGATES it with the arrow keys, and both go through the SAME `buildGitGraphCore()` - navigation here is VISUAL, so it moves by the layout @gitgraph actually produced (`getRenderedData()` gives every commit an `x` and `y`), never by an order re-derived beside the renderer. That re-derivation is the bug this replaced: @gitgraph orders its columns by when each branch first appears in ITS OWN iteration, so a hand-written lane list sent Left/Right to a column somewhere else on screen and the user lost their place. In its coordinates a column IS a branch (each gets a distinct `x`) and `y` GROWS DOWNWARD with age, so the newest commit is at the top of its column. **Each axis answers one question:** Up/Down walk `commitsInColumn` - the anchor's OWN column, newest first - and Left/Right are the only keys that cross to another branch, landing on the commit nearest the CURRENT HEIGHT (ties resolve upward) so the selection moves sideways instead of teleporting to some branch's tip. Do NOT move vertically by the global commit order: that drifts sideways on its own, which both leaves Left/Right with nothing to do and makes Home/End/PageUp/PageDown land on a branch the user never asked for (they are column ends too, via `gitGraphColumnEdge` / `jumpGitGraphVertical`, and a jump CLAMPS to the end where a single step holds). A commit's message carries the same `onClick` as its dot (`onMessageClick`), because the 5px dot is otherwise the only target and clicking the text a user is reading does nothing. `GitGraphView` also scrolls the selection back into view, since a 200-commit graph runs well past the viewport in both axes and an unseen selection is indistinguishable from a dead key. **The @gitgraph core must NOT know which commit is selected:** `<Gitgraph>` reads its `graph` prop only in its constructor, so a core rebuilt per keypress remounts the SVG, and the momentarily empty scroll container has its offsets clamped to zero - the graph snaps back to the newest commit on every arrow key and the whole feature reads as broken. Selection rides a context consumed by the view's own `renderDot`, and the `<Gitgraph>` key is derived from the core's CONTENTS so new commits still remount. That dot renderer is also what makes a commit clickable at all: it draws a transparent hit circle several times the 5px dot, concentric with it at `(dot.size, dot.size)`, which is where @gitgraph offsets the branch paths to. Note the graph is built from `git log --all` while the List view holds only the current branch, so graph navigation must move by COLUMN rather than by the list's index - a commit selected on a side branch has no list index, and the key reads as dead.

## Markdown, text, and fonts

- **Render markdown:** `<Markdown preset="chat | document | wizard-bubble | release-notes">` from `src/renderer/components/Markdown/` (do NOT hand-roll `<ReactMarkdown>` + a per-surface `components`/plugin map). The chat preset is what `MarkdownRenderer` wraps. Shared internals: `buildMarkdownPlugins` (`Markdown/plugins.ts`), `preprocessMarkdown` (`Markdown/preprocess.ts`), leaf renderers in `Markdown/components/*`, and the document component map `createMarkdownComponents()` in `src/renderer/utils/markdownConfig.ts`. See [UI-PATTERNS.md → `<Markdown>`](UI-PATTERNS.md).
- **Markdown to plain text (list rows, previews):** `stripMarkdown()`, `flattenMarkdownTables()` in `src/shared/markdown.ts`. `stripMarkdown` drops table grids outright, because raw `| col | col |` pipes render as garbage in a list row.
